# Evidence log

If these findings help your project, we would be happy about a mention, for example: "Protocol knowledge from TI Revive (https://github.com/derpsn/access-virus-ti-revive)". Corrections and new observations are welcome as issues, so everyone benefits.

Every conclusion gets an entry. Pointer = capture file + frame number(s), or binary (SHA256 prefix) + function address, or file + line/offset.
Confidence: HIGH (directly observed) / MED (strong inference) / LOW (plausible, needs confirmation).

| E033 | 2026-10-10 | Preset switching after sync (Ableton, 180 s, user switched in plugin, on hardware, and ping-pong). Plugin switch: host EP 0x06 c2 Bank Select `B0 00 00`, `B0 20 01`, Program Change `C0 pp` (t=16.8 0C, 41.8 47, 48.2 57, 53.9 67), then ~0.3 s later host c3 Single Dump to edit buffer `F0 00 20 33 01 10 10 00 00 0C 00 02 pp ...` (~524+ bytes). Hardware switch: device EP 0x86 c2 `F0 00 20 33 01 10 72 00 21 pp F7` (multi param 0x21 = program pp; t=37.8 38, 45.5 48, 51.7 58, 56.1 68, 56.6 69, 57.1 6A) + c3 `74 00 33 3A`, then ~0.3 s later host pushes the cached Single Dump for pp to the edit buffer. The plugin never requests a dump after a switch: it writes its synced copy (explains why sync is required). Corrected (per-event recount, usbdecode.py --events): 6 plugin switches (PC 03, 0C, 37, 47, 57, 67; 03 and 37 shared a transfer with F5/F8 and were dropped by a per-line filter) and 6 hardware switches (38, 48, 58, 68, 69, 6A) -> 12 dumps, every dump has a trigger. All 12 dumps are exactly 524 bytes (earlier 524-560 was a counting error, reassembly is correct). After each dump the host also sends on c2 CC 113 (`B0 71 vv`, often two values), Poly Pressure `A0 12/13/15/7A vv` and/or `F0 .. 6E 00 02 vv F7` (meaning unknown; Virus convention would be page B via poly pressure, page C via SysEx 6E: unverified) | captures/p2-preset-3.pcapng (addr 23) | usbdecode.py + event correlation | HIGH (sequence); MED (cable/command semantics) |

| E035 | 2026-10-10 | Raw pipe channel used by the plugin (handlers FUN_f1008f70..f1009a80): 0x220088 OPEN (caller output buffer exactly 0x8020 B: driver MDL-locks it as receive ring 0x8000 + header; header+0x8010 = user event HANDLE (ObReferenceObjectByHandle, signalled on RX), +0x8018 flag; exclusive per FileObject, 2nd opener gets 0xC0000038; needs pipes at dev+0x2480 (IN) and +0x2488 (OUT), starts 4 transfer contexts), 0x220084 WRITE (copy into 0x8000 TX ring, FUN_f1009740 sends up to 0x400 bytes per URB verbatim to the OUT pipe: no packetizing, so the plugin itself builds the 4-byte USB-MIDI packets incl. cable numbers), 0x22008C flush RX ring, 0x220090 CLOSE, 0x22007C/0x220080 wMaxPacketSize of IN/OUT pipe, 0x2200A8 TX ring fill/free, 0x2200AC flush TX ring. Pipes are presumably EP 0x86/0x06 (plugin traffic, E031/E033). Virus Control.dll uses 31 codes at 36 call sites incl. 0x220008 and 0x22007C-0x220090, 0x2200A8/AC/D4 (not used by the ASIO DLL). 0x220408/0x220410 (CORRECTED by E036: not VirusUSB IOCTLs but standard USB hub IOCTLs sent to the root hub) | VirusUSB.sys decompile FUN_f10095e0/f1009090/f1009270/f1008f70/f1009740/f1009ee0; Virus Control.dll call sites 0x180103c60..0x1801070e9 | Ghidra via REA, objdump + IAT scan | HIGH (semantics from code); MED (pipe = EP 0x06/0x86 mapping, 0x2204xx meaning) |

| E040 | 2026-10-10 | Vendor control requests through WinUSB: interface-recipient requests (0x41/0xC1) reach the device with wIndex rewritten to the interface number (4), so the flash protocol breaks (IN stalls, error 31); device-recipient requests (0x40/0xC0) keep wIndex and the device accepts them. Status (IN 0x81 wIndex 1) is a progress counter 00, 02 .. 10 (0x10 = ready; original driver: 155x 00 and 2..12x each of 02..10 in E026), ~12-17 polls of 10 ms per block. With 0x40/0xC0: FF7FC0 -> "FLSH200801010000", FF7FD0 -> "01", 800040 -> 00 00 00 02 1B DB C2 60 22 FF FF 02 32 88 FF FF: identical to the original driver's reads (E026). | E112 | 2026-10-12 | Mac: the Virus stuck without USB audio (device channels 0-3 exactly 0, 4/5 constant 0x07ff on every frame), not fixed by replugging or a stream restart, only by power-cycling the Virus. Trigger found in libVirusUSB logs: in a running GarageBand session (pid 40062, audio fine) a SECOND process using the Virus (an auval run, pid 40148) opened the device and did its own start-up stream restart (rate 44100->48000->44100 at 6717.95); from the next 5 s window on GarageBand's input showed the stuck pattern until the power cycle. (A second instance does not always open the device: in a controlled test the original refused because another instance was active, so the effect depends on timing.) The Access driver allowed one program at a time (Windows E048). Fix: vtibroker gives the Virus to one client (= one program, libVirusUSB keeps one connection per process) at a time: the first client is the owner, others get STATE 0 ("no Virus TI connected") and their writes/control requests are refused; when the owner disconnects the next client becomes owner. The installer no longer stops a running broker (that cut off a live session). Confirmed with the 0.6.1 test package while Ableton played: vtiautest (20 s) and auval (0 warnings) both got "waiting: another program uses the Virus" / device absent, Ableton's audio continued unchanged (ch 2/3 peaks ~0x0c00, 4/5 = 0) **[Later: E048 is one instance per process, not one program at a time; see (E035, E148).]** | /tmp/tirevive-libvirususb.log, /tmp/tirevive-vtibroker.log | src/broker/vtibroker_posix.c, src/installer-mac/scripts/postinstall | HIGH |

| E128 | 2026-10-12 | Occasional Virus sync reports on Linux (item 5): broker -v now logs the margin of every F5 time stamp against the synth's frame counter and each SYNC REPORT, plus USB/tick gaps. Findings: the broker is never late (USB blocks and tick sends max 10.1-10.8 ms every second); a report coincides with a single ~20-30 ms stall on the WINE side, after which the plug-in re-anchors its time stamps by ~1300 frames (30 ms) - the synth reports that jump. The stall freezes the whole plug-in process (MIDI writes paused 48 ms at the same moment) and happens ~30 s after the plug-in process starts: wineserver saves the registry (system.reg 3.2 MB, file mtime = stall time) because Wine rewrites the display adapter values (Class {4D36E968...} DriverDateData) at every process start; the save period is hard-coded (30 s), RegFlushKey is a no-op in Wine 10. Not every stall causes a report (2 of 4 runs). Mitigations tried: vtiaudio polls the ring every 1 ms under Wine instead of waiting for the socket tick + SetEvent (removes wineserver wake-up latency; ticks stay as fallback) - smooth otherwise (max gap 11 ms), the registry stall remains. Candidates: Wine 11 with ntsync (kernel 7.0 has /dev/ntsync) so events bypass wineserver; or accept (one short stall after load) | broker journal, vtiemu logs, reg mtimes | src/broker/vtibroker_posix.c (-v diagnostics), src/wrapper/vtiaudio.c (polling) | HIGH (cause) |

| E139 | 2026-10-14 | Stamp clock of the Virus (stage 3 R5, Linux broker ring): after the sync ping F0 00 20 33 01 10 73 00 22 00 F7 (stamped 3072, with FE + F2) the Virus emits ONE non-zero sample (value 511 in the 16-bit ring) on USB 2 (ring ch 4/5: R at frame f, L at f+1) ~800 frames after the ping is sent; channels 0/1 (where the decompile suggested) stay silent. At f the Virus' 14-bit F5 stamp clock is 0: an event stamped s sounds at ring frame f + s (mod 16384), exact to the frame in all honoured sessions. Stamps that ignore this land at a random point of that clock per session -> honoured with a random offset or played on arrival (+-10 ms) = the per-session latency spread 70..240 ms seen with ring-relative stamps. The native plug-in now converts stamps with the marker: identical K in 6/6 sessions, fixed reported latency. | E147 | 2026-10-15 | Project state grew by 45 bytes (3 records) per load/render/save cycle (reported in issue #5 on a TI Desktop; reproduced on the M3 with the TI2 via autest: 10374 -> 10419 -> 10464 -> 10509 bytes, 247 -> 250 -> 253 records). | E148 | 2026-10-15 | Issue #6 (Windows 11, TI Desktop, 0.8.4, Ableton 11): duplicating the track with the plug-in made the original instance's Virus play "a little bit behind" until the DAW was restarted. diag.log: the duplicate started fully connected (`state sent (... device connected)`, `device connected`, its own bank sync) and sent its own sync ping (`sync marker at frame 29970350`), while the original kept its marker 27253678 to the end; broker log: two `TI Revive Virus Control` clients at the same time. Mechanism (from code + the stamp clock in native/docs/protocol.md, not measured): the Virus has ONE 14-bit stamp clock and every ping resets it, so the original's stamps were off by (29970350 - 27253678) mod 16384 = 13312 frames from then on, and it never pings again. The per-note K values (klog) were too ambiguous to show the shift. Why a second instance got the Virus at all: the Windows broker shares the Virus between all clients (vtibroker.c, the ASIO driver is in the same process); only the macOS/Linux broker has one owner. Also seen: the stats line's "virus frames" was wrong with two instances (a function-local static shared by both). Fix (branch single-instance): one instance per process talks to the Virus, others wait and show "Virus in use / by another instance"; when the owner is removed the next takes over with start-up messages, state and a new ping. Hardware tests (Win11 VM, Ableton Live 11, TI Desktop, user + diag.log/klog): test 1 (CI run 38791531643) - the original stays in time next to a duplicate; the takeover sent 529 queued start-up messages while notes played, notes up to ~1.9 s late and no marker until playback stopped (~25 s; afterwards K 2559/2560 = the original's). Test 2 (CI run 38793336069, the takeover runs the start-up again: start page, 4 s note hold): the duplicate shows the original's "Virus unavailable" page (SystemStatus/Error 2), the takeover plays in time after the hold (user: "It works now"). Remaining: the first ping after a takeover still went unanswered, the marker came ~13 s later (retry) | issue #6 log (TI-Revive-logs-2026-10-15-1932.txt) | native/plugin/source/virusplugin.cpp, src/broker/vtibroker.c | HIGH (fix), MEDIUM (mechanism) |

| E150 | 2026-10-15 | Soft knobs ("Softknobs/Softknob 1..3", Easy page) did nothing in the native plug-in (issue #4, nasguile, Snow; the same on every TI: the parameter exists only in the editor layout, neither remotedevice.xml nor editordevice.xml defines it, `vtimodel encode` finds no transport). How the original does it (static analysis with REA/Ghidra of the user's Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64, sha256 a622f9a2...): a CSoftknobManager (constructor 0x1800a2f70) creates three parameters "Softknob 1..3" (0..127) with one instance per part (16) and keeps the SoftknobMapping list as a map destination number -> parameter. Each instance watches its part's "Soft Knobs/Destination N" (0x1800a28c0): when it changes, it looks the number up, takes that parameter's instance of the SAME part as its target, copies the target's range, value list and value and repaints; when the target changes (Virus panel, automation) it repaints too. Reading the knob returns the target's value (0x1800a2890), turning it sets the target's value first (0x1800a2840). So a soft knob is a pure proxy of its target, no scaling; destination 0 ("Dummy/Do Nothing") = no target. Native plug-in now the same (VirusPlugin::softknob_target, RemoteDevice::softknob_parameter; editor range/text/default and repaint dependency from the target). Test (Linux, TI Desktop, nativehost --editor under Xvfb, VTI_NATIVE_DEBUG): Init patch -> soft knobs inactive (destination 0); Common page Destination 1 = 2 -> dragging soft knob 1 set "Internal MIDI Standard Parameters/02 Breath Controller" of part 1 (4..38) and sent 16 control changes, the knob shows the value. | E152 | 2026-10-16 | (1) A preset chosen on the Virus itself did nothing while the native plug-in was connected (since 0.8.0): the Virus display showed the new name, but the sound, the plug-in and the soft-knob destinations stayed those of the old edit buffer ("-INIT-"). User test on the TI2 (Linux, REAPER with the installed plug-in, moduleinfo 0.8.3; the bug is in every 0.8.x): "changing presets on the TI2 does nothing on the plugin. always the same sound". Traffic (read-only broker observer): per step the Virus reports `Multi Part Parameter/Patch` (`72 00 21 pp`, cable 2) or `/Bank`, then `Internal System Info/Action Reference Value = 58` (`74 00 33 3A`, cable 3), and waits - in plug-in mode it does not load the patch itself (E033: the original then writes its cached Single Dump into the edit buffer). How the original does it (REA/Ghidra, Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64): CRemoteDevice (constructor 0x18006ce00, listener sub-object at +0x10 with vtable 0x18016aac8, method 0x180081980) listens to Multi Part Parameter/Bank and /Patch (pointers set up in 0x18006ed70); a change of a part's Bank or Patch marks that part with a time stamp (an earlier different pending part is loaded at once); its timer 0x18007ec30 calls 0x180083ae0(part) 150 ms after the last change. That function looks up (bank, patch) in its sound library: found -> the stored dump, re-addressed to the part's edit buffer (bytes 7/8 = 0/part, checksums), goes out, then it re-sends the part's controllers 1..16 (mod wheel, breath, ...); not found -> it requests that edit buffer from the Virus (`30 00 <part>`). Native plug-in now: panel_message marks the part on a reported Bank/Patch change (only after the readiness probe, only when the value differs), the sender thread's idle hook loads it 150 ms later with load_patch from the bank cache (RAM A-D = Bank 0-3, ROM A-Z = 4-29, same numbering as the Virus' "Val Location RAM/ROM"). Not done: the edit-buffer request when the patch is not in the cache (logged instead), the controller re-send. Test (Linux, TI2, nativehost with the plug-in, 2026-10-16): presets switched on the TI2 -> "preset chosen on the Virus, part 1 bank 1 patch 90..92: loaded from the bank cache", a Single Dump to the edit buffer ~150-200 ms after each report, the display shows the patch's soft-knob destinations again, the editor shows the new patch (user: "display shows the destinations now"). (2) Soft knobs turned on the Virus: standalone (no plug-in) the TI2 sends them as plain controllers on cable 1 (RinkDingAV: knob 1 Mutate = CC 13, knob 2 Reso = CC 3, knob 3 Length = CC 6, i.e. the patch's own CC assignment); in plug-in mode it reports only `Global Soft Knob Configuration/Value Softknob0..2` (Bank F 73, ids 10..12, 0..127), no change of the destination parameter - and does NOT apply it to the sound (CORRECTED by E154: the claim "it applies the change itself" here was an assumption, the test below checked only the editor). The original (0x180081980, part 0 of the global): reads `Multi Parameters/Panel Part` and sets soft knob N of that part to the value (0x118 setter of its Softknob 1..3 parameters at CRemoteDevice +0xcce90/98/a0), i.e. through the proxy (E150) the destination parameter. Native plug-in now the same: the reported value becomes the destination parameter's value of the panel part (state, editor, host parameter); "nothing is sent back" was wrong, see E154. Test (same setup, editor on the desktop): RinkDingAV, soft knobs 1-3 turned on the TI2 -> the soft knobs and their targets move in the editor (user: "all works"); 193 value reports, the plug-in sent nothing to the Virus meanwhile except clock. ctest green (Linux). | E154 | 2026-10-16 | Hardware tests of the E152 follow-ups (Linux, TI2, REAPER with the branch build in ~/.vst3, traffic with virusmon). (1) Soft knobs turned on the Virus: in plug-in mode the Virus does NOT apply them itself - user: "turning the softknobs does nothing to the sound" while the plug-in received `Value Softknob0..2` and sent nothing. The original (REA): 0x180081980 calls slot 0x118 of its CSoftknobHandler (vtable 0x18016e788, slot -> 0x180067960 -> the part instance's setter), the soft knob instance's setter 0x1800a2840 first calls the target's setter (slot 0x130) with the value unchanged, i.e. the destination parameter is set and sent like any parameter change, no scaling (the soft knob parameters are 0..127, created in 0x1800a2f70). Native plug-in now: the reported value, clamped to the target's range, goes to the panel part's destination parameter and is sent to the Virus (one message per report, ~19/s while turning, answered within ~1 ms). User: "the sound changes now with the softknobs". (2) Preset chosen on the Virus, patch in the bank cache (RAM, and ROM from firmware.bin): fast, sound and name right (user: "ROM presets load fast and softknobs work"); after each load the 13 controllers of 0x180083ae0 follow within ~30 ms with the known values. (3) Patch NOT in the cache (forced: VTI_ACCESS_DIR = a folder with only a link to Virus Control.dll, so no firmware.bin and no ROM banks; ROM banks chosen on the TI2): the edit-buffer request `30 00 00` is answered with the chosen ROM patch (names match the selection, ~1 s per reply), but in plug-in mode the Virus keeps playing the previous sound. While it answers it holds its panel reports back and sends them in a burst right after the reply (Bank 5..17 within 1 s after a reply), so a reply is often stale; writing it back overwrote the edit buffer with an older patch ("jumping back"), and a later read returned that write. With a stale check (300 ms grace, write back only if Bank/Patch unchanged) the right patch was written back, but the Virus still did not change the sound (user: "name fits but sound never changes") - unexplained, the bytes of the reply vs a cache dump were not compared. Decision: the read-back on a cache miss is switched off (the part is left alone and logged, as before); a normal installation has the ROM banks from firmware.bin, so a miss only happens before the first RAM sync or without firmware.bin. Open: why the Virus ignores a read-back dump (compare raw bytes, e.g. bytes 10..12 which the original rewrites in 0x180083ae0 for library patches) | user tests on the TI2 in REAPER, virusmon traffic, REA static analysis (addresses above) | native/plugin/source/virusplugin.cpp (panel_message, load_reported_presets, fetch_edit_buffer), native/src/model/panelfollow.* | HIGH (1), (2); open (3) |

Linux, TI2, REAPER with a local experiment build (read-back switched on, not committed) and VTI_ACCESS_DIR = a folder with only a link to Virus Control.dll (no firmware.bin -> no ROM banks), traffic with `virusmon --hex`. Sequence: RAM-D "AbstractRD" loaded from the cache and sent; then ROM-A chosen on the TI2 -> edit buffer request `30 00 00` -> reply named "3-Dumm  HS" (the real name of ROM-A 1), but its sound data equals the "AbstractRD" dump the plug-in had sent: 35 differing bytes = byte 9 (format), 11/12 (Original Bank 4 / Patch 0), byte 59, the name 249..258, the checksums 264/265 and the newer-format fields; against ROM-A 1 from firmware.bin 196 bytes differ. Choosing ROM bank 5 next returned the same sound data under the name "AcidBath M". So in plug-in mode a program chosen on the panel changes only the name and the origin bytes in the edit buffer, not the sound - the host has to send the patch (E152), and a read-back cannot supply it. User: "name changed but sound doesnt ... only the name changed". Native plug-in: the read-back code is removed; a patch missing from the cache is left alone with a log line (ROM: needs firmware.bin next to Virus Control; RAM: not synced yet). The FX 1 page of the editor layout binds 67 parameters with three transports from remotedevice.xml (the original reads the same file): CC (Chorus, Input Follower), poly pressure "pp" (Phaser, EQ, Analog Boost, Distortion type/intensity) and SysEx Bank C/D 6E/6F (Filter Bank, Distortion mix/quality/tone/treble/high cut, Input). Measured on our TI2 (Linux, native plug-in in nativehost, Init patch, one C4 held from 6 s, parameter changes through process() like host automation, Out 1 recorded, virusmon traffic): Phaser Mix 0 -> 127 at 9 s (pp): the stereo difference L-R jumps from 0 to 468 in the same 250 ms window; EQ Low Gain 64 -> 127 at 13 s (pp): level peaks 1340 -> 2270 at once; A-Boost Intensity -> 127 at 17 s (pp): peaks 2270 -> 2800 at once; Filter Bank Filter Type -> 127 at 21 s (SysEx 6E, Filter Bank on with mix 127 in Init): stereo difference 380 -> 1250 within 0.5 s. | E161 | 2026-10-16 | How the original holds notes at start-up and while it is busy (closes E153's open part). REA/Ghidra, Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64: every MIDI message from the host passes 0x18007aa50 (via CRemoteDeviceFromHostQueue 0x18006cc00); while the byte CRemoteDevice +0xccc49 is set, note-on, note-off (status 0x80/0x90) and the MIDI clock F8 are dropped - not delayed - while all other messages go on. The constructor (0x18006ce00) and the connection set-up in the timer 0x18007ec30 set +0xccc49/+0xccc4a to 1; on every timer tick 0x18007a9b0 sets +0xccc49 = busy, where busy = its library/sync object +0xccc90 is syncing (+0x548) or has requests open (+0x5d4 > 0), or the start-up queue +0xcd058 still holds messages, or the flags +0xcd0f0 / +0x136580 are set; when busy turns on, it sends All Notes Off (B0..BF 7B 00) on all 16 channels. So the original plays nothing while it works through its start-up messages, a bank sync or dump requests. The native plug-in instead holds notes back until the readiness probe is answered (E149) and pauses its bank sync while the host plays (README: "you can play while it runs") - kept that way on purpose: no lost notes, same start-up safety | REA static analysis (addresses above) | native/plugin/source/virusplugin.cpp (wait_virus_ready, the note hold, the sync pause) | HIGH |

| E167 | 2026-10-16 | How the original sends host audio to the Virus' USB input ("3 Outs / 1 Input", follow-up of E164). (1) VST3 shell (Virus TI.vst3 x64, sha256 0cd8d04e...): it declares the audio inputs "Dummy In" (bus 0, main) and "Sidechain" (bus 1, aux) only when the VST2 plug-in inside has more than one input (initialize 0x180002e40); its process (0x1800032e0, IAudioProcessor vtable 0x18001cd78 slot 9) passes the channel buffers of input bus 1 = Sidechain (only if the host supplies at least 2 input buses and the VST2 side has 2 inputs) as the VST2 inputs 0/1; "Dummy In" is never read; missing buffers are replaced by silent ones. (2) Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64 (sha256 a622f9a2...): the VST2 variant table 0x1801c52f0 gives 2 inputs / 6 outputs; processReplacing (AEffect stub 0x180001140 -> 0x18002fd20 -> 0x18002fd40 -> 0x18007a870) hands the inputs unchanged to the audio device's host-side process (0x1800aa7c0, vtable 0x18016f178 slot +0xd0). There, per device output channel (count at device +0x30, the 2 channels of the OUT stream, E082), VST2 input i is written into channel i's sample-rate converter (CSampleRateConverter, created in 0x1800aa1c0, ring >= 256 frames) when the flag at device +0xa6a is set and the host buffer exists; otherwise silence is written. The device callback (0x1800a8f60) drains these converters into the OUT stream with a fill-level-driven rate correction (the same scheme as the input side). So in the original the Sidechain bus is the audio that reaches the Virus' USB input. (3) +0xa6a and the third-USB flag +0xa69 are handed to the driver at connect (0x1800a6c70 -> 0x1800fe8c0: commands 0x21 and 0x1f); which code sets +0xa6a was not found in the device class setters - that it follows IO configuration 2 is an inference. (4) Choosing "3 Outs / 1 Input" shows a warning (0x18002e170): the mode disables the Virus as the system sound card, "only use this mode when side chaining inputs is required"; in Cubase below version 9000 it shows "This input/output configuration is not supported" instead. | E178 | 2026-10-16 | What the Access driver does with IN packets of the wrong size for a long time (REA/Ghidra 12.1.4 on the user's VirusUSB.sys x64, sha256 ea5426ac...): the frame-pattern observer (FUN_f1027750, called from the IN completion FUN_f10209b0) replaces every packet outside the nominal count +- tolerance with silence of the pattern length and counts these packets in an error counter that is reset once the pattern locks again ("FramePattern Locked - reset nErrorCounter"). The IN completion has a recovery: above 40 counted errors it calls a check, and if that fails or above 320 errors it stops the stream and signals "irqlOnStreamBroken" (FUN_f1023cb0) - but only for devices in two vendor/product lists (FUN_f1018060, FUN_f1017930) or 0a4a:6a02 / 0a4a:f1a0 (other devices of the same driver framework); neither list contains the Access vendor 0x133e. So for the Virus TI the original driver only fills silence, no stream restart, no SET_CUR re-send: our repair matches it, and the original would not have recovered from the E175/E176 state either (consistent with E112: only a power cycle). | E196 | 2026-10-17 | Remote templates of the original, sending and storage (Virus Control 5.1.7, sha256 a622f9a2...; follows E193). Records (byte offsets in the SysEx message): name record 28 bytes = F0 00 20 33 01 10 13 <t> 20 <name, 16 chars at 9..24> <flags at 25: bit 0 = MIDI Channel Behaviour "Enable MIDI Channel"> 00 F7; knob record 65 bytes = F0 00 20 33 01 10 13 <t> <knob 0..31> <name, 10 chars at 9..18> <Knob Behaviour 0..127: low nibble at 19, high nibble at 20> 21..22 <MIDI string, 40 bytes at 23..62: each MIDI byte as two bytes (low nibble, high nibble), 0F 0F = the knob's value, ended by 0E 0F when there is room; default = just 0E 0F = empty> 00 F7; defaults blank names, behaviour 0 (data at 0x1801c59e0). An older 27-byte name record without the flags byte is accepted and converted (receiver 0x18008b8d0). The plug-in owns the 32 templates: CRemoteSets (0x1800899b0) loads them at start-up from the cache file {common_appdata}/Access Music/Virus TI/Cache/RemoteSets (0x18008ab00) and writes all 32 back when the plug-in is destroyed (writer 0x18008ad50, called from the CRemoteDevice destructor 0x18006d480). Cache format, checked on a real cache written by the original under Wine (71688 bytes): "MIDI", 00 01 18 00 = big-endian total payload length, then each SysEx message with a big-endian 32-bit length in front (32 name + 1024 knob records, template t = name record then its 32 knob records). Sending (value handler 0x18008b020): choosing a template ("Remote Templates/Selected Template") sets the Virus parameter "Global Patch Select/Remote Patch" to the index and sends that template's name record and all 32 knob records (0x18008c4c0); editing the template name or MIDI Channel Behaviour sends the name record; editing a knob's name, Knob Behaviour or MIDI String sends that knob record; "Knob Template" (list at 0x1801c57b0, e.g. "B0 05 FF") fills the MIDI String. Records go to the Virus on USB-MIDI cable 3 (capture, c3), not cable 2 like parameters. Capture remote-page.pcapng: only three name records of template 0 (flags 00, 01, 00 = MIDI Channel Behaviour off/on/off), no knob records and no "Remote Patch" change, so the clicks on templates 1 and 5 did not change "Selected Template" there (why: open). The Virus answered nothing. MIDI string text (parser 0x1800893f0, slot 0x200 of CMIDIStringParameterInstance, vtable 0x18016bd18): upper-cased; two hex digits = one byte, a lone hex digit = that value, "*" / "X" / "V" (a run counts once) = the value, "%n" = a decimal byte (clamped 0..255), spaces and punctuation skipped, at most 20 bytes; shown back as two hex digits per byte and "*" for the value ("b0 7 x" -> "B0 07 *"). Open: where the Virus keeps received records (RAM or flash; rule 2), the template files' format (Personal Remote Templates), MIDI String Info, the Knob Template list (table 0x1801c57b0) | REA/Ghidra static analysis, USB capture, the original's cache file (Wine prefix, read-only) | native/src/model/remotetemplates.* (not changed yet) | HIGH (record layout, cache format, when it sends, MIDI string encoding), open (Virus-side storage) |
| E197 | 2026-10-17 | The REMOTE page's Knob Template list of the original (Virus Control 5.1.7): 35 entries (parameter "Remote Templates/Knob Template", range 0..34, set up in 0x180089bf0) from a static table of (menu path, MIDI string) pointer pairs at 0x1801c57b0 (16 bytes per entry, ends at 0x1801c59e0 right before the default name record); paths use "/" for submenus (4 top-level groups), every MIDI string is hex text with FF where the knob's value goes. Choosing one sets the selected knob's MIDI String to that text (0x18008b020), which the parser turns into the record bytes (E196). In the Mac bundle binary (universal, x86_64 + i386) the same list lies elsewhere (not before the name record). Ours: find_knob_templates reads it at runtime from the user's own binary by shape (the first run of at least 8 pointer pairs whose targets are a printable name and "XX XX ..." hex text, resolved through the PE sections or the 64-bit Mach-O segments), nothing of it is stored in the repo; found in the Windows DLL (35 entries, 27 ms) and in the Mac binary (35 entries, the same list by hash). | E202 | 2026-10-17 | The 32-bit Virus Control.dll as the Access installation (round 0.8.22c test W2, win11 VM, Ableton Live 11): the REMOTE page's Knob Template arrow opened nothing and only the MIDI String field reacted (it is a text field, as in the original). The VM's `C:\Program Files\Access Music\Virus TI\Common\Virus Control.dll` (5.1.7.00, 14040312 bytes) is a PE32 i386 DLL, not the PE32+ x64 DLL of this PC's installation (14663928 bytes); our finder (E197) only read 64-bit pointer pairs and found 0 templates in it (wavetables, found without pointers, 4096 in both). The 32-bit DLL holds the same table as (name, MIDI string) pairs of 4-byte absolute pointers (image base + RVA, 8 bytes per entry). Fix: the PE address map records the pointer size from the optional header (0x10B PE32: image base at +28, 4-byte pointers; 0x20B PE32+: +24, 8 bytes) and find_knob_templates reads pairs of that size. Result on the user's two DLLs (local, read-only, scratchpad only): 35 templates each, 35 of 35 entries identical; unit test accessfiles_knob_templates_pe32 (synthetic PE32). | E203 | 2026-10-17 | Offline render (export, freeze, offline bounce). (1) The original refuses it: the VST3 shell (Virus TI.vst3 x64, sha256 0cd8d04e..., setupProcessing 0x180003a70, IAudioProcessor vtable 0x18001cd78 slot 7) stores the host's processMode and passes block size and rate to the VST2 side; Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64 (sha256 a622f9a2...) answers canDo "offline" and "noRealTime" with "no" (0x18002d8c0); its processReplacing (0x18002fd40) asks the host's process level and, when it is offline (4), arms a flag that makes the idle routine (0x180080ba0) show "Offline rendering or freezing to disk of Virus TI tracks is not supported. Please use the realtime rendering functionality provided by your host to avoid noise and crackle." once, and returns without rendering that block. (2) The native plug-in before this change: an offline host calls process() faster than real time, the timeline runs ahead of the audio ring and re-anchors, the read position clamps to the newest frames (repeated/missing audio; inferred from the code, virusplugin.cpp process(), not measured). (3) Change: OfflinePacer (native/src/device/offlinepace.h) holds each offline block (ProcessData::processMode == kOffline, set per block by the AU wrapper too) until the ring has the block's frames; the timeline runs on the paced frame; a start wait (Virus connecting, start-up probe) once per render, then the anchor in that block; 1 s timeout per block. Measured on Linux, TI2 Desktop, nativehost --offline (new option), C4 every second: real time -> offline at 10 s (44.1 kHz, 512): 10.00 s of audio in 9.97 s wall time, offline note onsets 10/10 at 49.37 ms (same as the real-time part), timeline err constant; offline from the start (48 kHz, 256): 22.9 s wall for 16 s (start wait ~7 s), 1 anchor, marker found, onsets 1-15 s at 43.96-43.98 ms (the note at 0.0 s falls into the start-up hold). | E205 | 2026-10-17 | The original's sync error page (Virus Control 5.1.7 x64, sha256 a622f9a2...; follows E072). Its advice for sync errors: a message (0x180169dc0) warns that a device sharing the USB root hub with the Virus TI "might cause sync errors and audible artefacts" and asks to use another USB port (the README/site advice "a USB port the Virus does not share with other devices" quotes this). The skin's Info Panel shows "A sync error occurred. Press Stop and Play to clear." for the editor parameter "Info Panel/Page" = 1 (editordevice.xml condition "Info Panel/Show Alert Sync Error"; page 2 = "Buffersize not power of 2"). Init (0x18006ed70) binds "Info Panel/Page" (object +0x1365b0), "Global USB/Sync Information Event" (+0x136590), "Debug/Sync Events", "Debug/Sync Events after Play", "Debug/Sync Events Trigger Level" (+0x136598/+0x1365a0/+0x1365a8) and "VirusControl/Live Mode" (+0x1368f8). The parameter listener (0x180081980, called with the object + 0x10): on Sync Information Event = 1 it adds 1 to the total and the since-Play counters and 20 to a level (+0x1368f0); when the level parameter changes, the host is playing (+0x136900) and Live Mode is 0, a level above 60 sets Info Panel/Page = 1 (only from 0). The idle routine (0x180080ba0, called from the host idle 0x18002df10 and the GUI-thread process path) lowers the level by 1 per call and applies a pending clear. Transport start (0x180081570, playing = true) sets the clear flag (page 0) and zeroes the since-Play counter and the level; Stop alone keeps the page. Our model decodes F0 00 20 33 01 10 73 00 54 01 F7 to that global (test access_sync_information_event). Native plug-in before: never read the event, never showed the page. Now: SyncAlert (native/src/model/syncalert.h, 6 tests), events from panel_message, tick every 50 ms on the worker (the original ticks per host idle call: its rate depends on the host, 50 ms is our choice), transport from process(); vtirender with Info Panel/Page=1 draws the red bar in the info panel. The trigger was not provoked on hardware (rule 2) | Virus Control.dll 0x18006ed70, 0x180081980, 0x180080ba0, 0x180081570, 0x18002df10; editordevice.xml Conditionsets | REA/Ghidra on the user's own Access files; vtitests; vtirender --set "Info Panel/Page=1" | HIGH (behaviour), MED (tick rate) |

| E213 | 2026-10-18 | Cause of the E212 Linux sleep FAIL: at wake the broker lost the Virus and reopened it 0.27 s later (journal: 09:30:10.889 "usbaudio: IN resubmit failed", "read ep 86: LIBUSB_ERROR_NO_DEVICE", "device failed: closing", "usbaudio: stopped ... stalled 5000 ms"; 09:30:11.157 "device open, handshake sent"; no "audio on" afterwards). The plug-in's worker polls the broker's present flag and never saw it absent, so it kept audio_ok_ = 1 and never asked the broker to start the audio of the new device: the shm ring's streaming flag stayed 0, newest_frame() -1 (stats "host frames 0 / virus frames -1, last sound -748776 frames ago"), no anchor, no ping, no marker, silent; the StallWatch line in the same second came from the old stream's last frames. E211 passed because there the Virus was absent long enough to be seen (reconnect path). Fix (round-0.8.24): the broker client counts arrivals (STATE 1 after anything else, Broker::device_arrivals) and the worker's PresenceWatch treats a changed count while present as gone + back (log "the Virus was gone for a moment - reconnecting"): audio restart, start-up, state, new timeline. Tests broker_counts_device_arrivals, presencewatch_* (214 tests pass). | E221 | 2026-10-18 | A Live 11 project saved with the ORIGINAL plug-in (VST3, class id - corrected below - D9909FB45F730543AC5397BE8958CD31; work/projcompat/restore-test-vst3.als, made on the win10 VM) does NOT open with TI Revive in Ableton Live 11.3.43 (win11 VM, TI Revive 0.8.24 installed, no Access plug-in): Live says "Das Plugin VST3 konnte nicht gefunden werden"; its Log.txt: "VST3: Going to restore: Virus TI" / "error: VST3: Restore 1 failed: Virus TI". Our installed bundle carries Contents/Resources/moduleinfo.json with "Compatibility": New B49F90D9...CD6B (ours), Old [B49F90D9...CD31 (the original), ...CD94, bridge] - Live 11 does not use it and restores only by the exact class id. So the claim "projects made with the original open" (README, native/docs/architecture.md "Identity and old projects") holds only for hosts that read moduleinfo compatibility (VST 3.7.5+ hosts; which ones is untested) - not for Live 11. The plug-in state itself is compatible (the same project state loaded into TI Revive on Linux with nativehost: 244 records, part 1 Fimplex HS, getState identical). Live 12 (Mac) not yet tried; the VST2 project (original 'VCP ' 0x56435020) not yet tried in Live (we declare no VST2 id) Fix (round-0.8.25, user decision "second ID in the plug-in"): the factory registers the same plug-in a second time under the original's id as "TI Revive Virus Control (original projects)" (entry.cpp; not in the VTI_NATIVE_DROPIN build); moduleinfo compatibility unchanged (hosts that read it map the original to our main class). Test factory_classes (moduleinfotool: both ids); nativehost --cid B49F90D9735F4305AC5397BE8958CD31 loads the project's state through that class (Linux, dev build: 244 records, part 1 Fimplex HS, getState identical). Not yet tested in Live 11 (needs a Windows build). Known cost: a machine that still has the original Access VST3 installed has two plug-ins with that id CORRECTION (same day, test build 0.8.25a, CI run 39124817970): with the second class registered as B49F90D9735F4305AC5397BE8958CD31 Live 11 still failed ("Restore 1 failed: Virus TI", the class listed in its browser). Cause: that string was never the original's class id as a FUID. Live stores a VST3 class id as the FUID's four 32-bit words (Uid Fields): a project saved with TI Revive has B49F90D9 735F4305 AC5397BE 8958CD6B (= our id string), the original's project has D9909FB4 5F730543 AC5397BE 8958CD31. The old string came from our vtihost class listing, which printed the raw TUID bytes (B4 9F 90 D9 73 5F 43 05 ...; Windows TUIDs are COM-ordered: first word little-endian, then two little-endian halves). So VTI_CID_ORIGINAL, the moduleinfo compatibility entry and the VTI_NATIVE_DROPIN id were wrong since 0.7.x (a host that reads moduleinfo could not have mapped the original either); the wrapper id derived from the raw bytes (...CD6B) is ours and stays. Fixed (round-0.8.25): kOriginalClassId and VTI_CID_ORIGINAL = D9909FB45F730543AC5397BE8958CD31, factory_classes checks it, nativehost --cid D9909FB45F730543AC5397BE8958CD31 loads the project state (Linux). "Virus TI Snow" as a FUID: 1B4C380BDBEA67409E8EEEE233A98C9C (from its raw bytes; not registered by us, no Snow project to test) | Live Log.txt (win11 VM, C:\Users\vti\AppData\Roaming\Ableton\Live 11.3.43\Preferences), user report, moduleinfo.json of the installed bundle | checklist projcompat-1 PW1 | HIGH (Live 11 does not map) |

| E228 | 2026-10-18 | Firmware package of the user's Access installer: firmware.bin (8569757 bytes, sha256 8e3daea8...) is an IFF FORM whose TABL names 52 chunks: 43 LCD bitmaps lcd_*.bin (640 bytes each: backup/configuring/connected/flashrom/restore/update screens), vti.bin (TI, 86 sectors), vti_2.bin (TI2, 94), vti_snow.bin (Snow, 53), Changes.htm, Welcome.htm. vti_2.bin = FORM of 32 KB flash sectors F000-F013, F020-F035, F060/F061, P064-P121, S002-S009; a 35842-byte chunk = 512 records of (3-byte flash address, 32 data bytes, 3-byte address, 32 data bytes), addresses step 0x20 and are contiguous per sector, + 2 tail bytes (checksum, not decoded); the 35840-byte chunks (P064-P115 = ROM banks, E154) carry no addresses. Sector addresses: F000-F013 at 0x000000..0x0d0000 (64 KB steps, 32 KB each), F020-F035 at 0x140000..0x220000 (F034 and F035 both say 0x220000, contents differ), F060/F061 both 0x3c0000 (identical), P120/P121 at 0x780000/0x790000, S002-S009 at 0x820000..0x890000. Contents: F000 = code of the host CPU, an 8051-family part (vectors LJMP at 0/3/0x0B/0x23/0x2B/0x43/0x4B, RETI at 0x13; 614 RET; IE/TMOD/SBUF/PCA registers; version "5.1.7.00" at sector offset 0x3b); F001-F009 = more code and tables for it, reached through a 7-bit flash page register at XDATA 0x02E0 (shadow INTMEM 0x7A; function 0x5716 selects a page; page k = flash k*0x10000 appears at code 0x8000-0xFFFF; page 6 = F006 is the main code page: 168-entry jump table at its start, 553 functions together with the common area; F003/F008/F010-F013 are tables and UI text in length-prefixed records, e.g. "MIDI Buffer overflow", "DSP not booted!", "USB connection has been lost"); F020-F035 = DSP code (24-bit words; starts with a MOVEP to the PLL control register X:$FFFFBB, i.e. Motorola DSP56300-family boot code; no disassembler in Ghidra 12.1.4 or Hopper, not analysed); F060 = the firmware of the USB controller: 12-byte header (bytes 4-5 = 0x000c header length, 8-9 = 0x1d16 code length) + 7446 bytes of 8052 code (vector table at header end: LJMP 0x12d7, 0x1102, 0x1bf4, 0x13e7) + "tusb037.bin 10/13/10-13:33:00 (C)ACCESS MUSIC"; it holds the USB descriptors (VID 133e PID 0815 bcdDevice 0x0300; audio control + 2 streaming interfaces: EP 0x01 OUT 200 bytes 2 channels, EP 0x82 IN 592 bytes 6 channels, 16 bit, 44.1/48 kHz; vendor interface 3 with interrupt EP 0x05 OUT 8 / 0x85 IN 16 bytes; vendor interface 4 with interrupt EP 0x06 OUT 32 / 0x86 IN 32 bytes; bInterval 1) and drives memory-mapped registers at 0xFF80-0xFFFF with the interrupt vector register at 0xFFB2 = Texas Instruments TUSB3200A / TAS1020 family (8052 core, USB streaming controller: the audio streams are handled by this chip). Tools: own scripts in work/firmware/scripts (fwlist.py chunk list + sector extraction, pages.py page model + seeds, Seed8051.java/Export8051.java for Ghidra headless 8051:BE:16:default); REA 6.7.0 opens only ELF/PE/Mach-O of x86/ARM/MIPS/PowerPC/RISC-V/Hexagon, so Ghidra was run headless; all images, Ghidra projects and exports in work/firmware/ (git-ignored; Access material) **[Open: bcdDevice 0x0300 here, but the F060 descriptor tables say 0x0100 and a real TI2 reports 0x0300 (E388, E393).]** | static analysis of the user's firmware.bin | work/firmware/ | HIGH (layout, record format, page model, USB chip family from the register map and file name); MED (host CPU exact part open: 8051 with PCA at 0xDA-0xDE, a second serial channel at SFR 0x9A/0x9B and SFR 0x91/0x94-0x96 use); HIGH (DSP family from the boot code) |
| E229 | 2026-10-18 | USB controller <-> host CPU link and its flow control (static, same analysis as E228; USB side = F060 code, host side = F000 common area). USB side: the OUT endpoints' byte-count registers (0xFF83 for the small vendor endpoint, 0xFF7B for the 32-byte one; data at 0xFED0 / 0xFEE8) are read by the USB interrupt handler (0x0430); the packets are copied (0x18f1) into a 64-byte ring at 0xFE50 (INTMEM 0x5E read / 0x5F write index, masked 0x3F) only if at least 32 bytes are free, otherwise the count register is left set, so the chip keeps answering NAK and nothing is dropped on the USB side. The 8052 UART (SCON 0x92: mode 2, 9-bit, SMOD 0 = MCU clock/64) sends the ring 4 bytes per frame with the 9th bit set on the first byte (= one USB-MIDI event packet per frame, serial ISR 0x13e7) and appears to transmit only while the input T0 (P3.4) is high (decompiler output unclear, MED); received frames (9th bit = frame start) go to INTMEM 0x9E-0xA1. Host side: a serial channel at SFR 0x9A (status: bit 0 receive, bit 1 transmit, bit 2 received 9th bit, bit 3 9th bit to send) / 0x9B (data), ISR 0x3288 at vector 0x4B: a frame lands in INTMEM 0xC8-0xCB (0xC8 = cable << 4 \| CIN) and goes to 0x286d / 0x28e7; if fewer than 8 bytes are free in the destination ring the packet is parked (state 99, XDATA 0x04F7 counts (mod 128)) and taken by the main loop 0x14e4 once 12 bytes are free - a frame arriving meanwhile would replace it - but none arrives: the chip waits for the host's per-byte toggle (E239, corrected 2026-10-18: no loss here). Transmit: 4-byte packets from the queue XDATA 0x6C00-0x6FFF (256 entries in four 256-byte columns, write index INTMEM 0x3D, read 0x3E), 9th bit on the first byte; the host toggles P1.1 after each received frame (handshake candidate, open). Dispatch by cable (0x28e7, bytes per CIN as in the USB-MIDI class): 0 -> DIN MIDI out ring (0x281b); 3 -> the host's MIDI parser ring (0x277c); 5 -> straight to both DSPs (0x605b writes command 0x39 and the byte to the host ports at XDATA 0x0000-0x0007 and 0x0100-0x0107, no ready check); the remaining cables (2) -> parser ring and both DSPs. This matches E025/E033 (cable 2 channel messages, 3 SysEx, 5 F5 stamps) and says why F5 stamps never show in the host's own state: they go to the DSPs only. Rate: with the TUSB3200A's 12 MHz MCU clock (data sheet value, not in the code) the link runs at 187.5 kbaud, 11 bits per byte, i.e. about 4.2 k packets/s sustained, 16 packets buffered in the chip, then NAK  **Correction (simulator S005/S043, not re-checked by us):** the firmware writes GLOBCTL 0xC5, so the chip runs at 24 MHz and the link at 375 kbaud (about 8.5 k events/s), not the datasheet default 187.5 marked LOW here. | static analysis (Ghidra headless) | work/firmware/out/tusbcode.txt, bank00.txt | HIGH (ring, NAK, frame format, dispatch), MED (T0 gating), LOW (baud rate) |
| E230 | 2026-10-18 | Host CPU MIDI rings and the "MIDI Buffer overflow" behaviour (static, F000 common area). Three 1 KB rings in XDATA with 10-bit indices (low byte + 2-bit high byte in INTMEM, wrap at 4): 0x6000-0x63FF DIN MIDI out (filled by 0x281b, drained by the UART0 transmit interrupt in 0x2cd9; indices 0x40/41 write, 0x42/43 read), 0x6400-0x67FF MIDI in to the parser (filled by 0x277c from the DIN input - UART0 receive, 31250 baud: TH1 0xFC, timer 1 mode 2, SMOD 0 - and from USB cables 2/3; drained by 0x27b6 from the main loop 0x14e4; indices 0x35/36 write, 0x37/38 read), 0x6800-0x6BFF (filled by 0x24fe in the UART0's second receive mode, flag _6_2, probably thru; drained by 0x2548). When a write catches the read index (ring full) the ring is reset: indices to 1/0, byte 0xF7 at the ring start, flag set (_3_5 for 0x6400, _3_6 for 0x6000 and 0x6800) - everything queued is discarded and a running SysEx is cut; the flag is what later shows "MIDI Buffer overflow" (text in page 0x0a, referenced from a message table in page 4 at 0x383e; display path not followed, MED). The main loop runs the parser at most 8 ring bytes... per pass and polls the link state (parked packets, E229) between. Active Sensing (0xFE) from DIN is handled without queueing. The DSP side (one host-port command 0x39 per MIDI byte, no ready check) is where a flood would have to hurt - the audio hang of rule 2 cannot be read without a DSP56300 disassembler (open) | static analysis (Ghidra headless) | work/firmware/out/bank00.txt | HIGH (rings, reset on overflow), MED (which flag shows the message, thru ring), open (DSP side) |
| E231 | 2026-10-18 | Our send path against the firmware's limits (E229/E230; code reading, no test). Broker: vti_write splits a message into 32-byte interrupt transfers on EP 0x06 (wMaxPacketSize), 1 s timeout per transfer (libusb on Linux/macOS, WinUSB pipe policy on Windows); a timeout fails the whole write (logged, the rest of that message is dropped). Plug-in: all events of a block go out in one broker write of up to 3072 bytes (768 packets; fairness break after 8 queued messages), every stamped channel message is preceded by an F5 packet on cable 5 (a note = 2 packets), host automation is coalesced per parameter and limited to 250/s with a burst of 64 (AutomationQueue; the stress test that hung the Virus' audio was 1000/s plus ~2750 notes/s = about 6500 packets/s), SysEx dumps (memsync) are written one message at a time without pacing (the original sends dumps at about MIDI speed, protocol.md). Firmware side: the USB chip takes a 32-byte transfer only while at least 32 of its 64 ring bytes are free and otherwise NAKs, so a large write blocks the broker's write for that client until the UART has drained it - at the LOW-confidence 4.2 k packets/s a 3072-byte write is ~180 ms, 64 automation packets + their stamps ~30 ms; the host CPU parks a frame when its ring has fewer than 8 free bytes and loses it if the next frame arrives first. Reading: 250/s automation is far under the link rate; the hazard is the burst (automation burst + notes with stamps in one block) and sustained totals above ~4 k packets/s, where USB backpressure makes broker writes slow and the host CPU's parking rule starts dropping. Where the audio hang comes from is still not visible on the host-CPU side (E230: the DSP host-port writes are unchecked) - next: the DSP's host receive handler with the new disassembler (E232). No change made to the code **[Later: the link is lossless (toggle handshake): nothing is lost at the parking point (E239).]**  **Correction (with E229's):** at 375 kbaud the write times halve (3072 bytes about 90 ms, not about 180). | code reading of src/broker/vticore*.c, native/plugin/source/virusplugin.cpp, native/src/device/automation.h, memsync.cpp | - | HIGH (our side), LOW (link rate) |
| E232 | 2026-10-18 | DSP56300 disassembly is possible after all. (1) github.com/dsp56300/dsp56300 (GPL-3, the emulator core of gearmulator, commit of 2026-10-18) ships a standalone tool source/disassemble (dsp56kDisassemble: raw 3-byte big-endian words, symbols for the peripheral registers, caller lists); built here with cmake (asmjit submodule needed) - on the TI2 DSP image (sectors F020-F033 = 14 x 10922 words after dropping the 2 pad bytes per sector; F034 and F035 are shorter extra images, both addressed 0x220000, open which DSP/variant they serve) it gives a 126k-line listing with no unknown opcodes; the first words after a 3-word header (0e2ba9 000047 000100) are the DSP's init (BCR, SP, OMR, AAR0-3, HPCR = host port). (2) github.com/yatli/ghidra_dsp56k (SLEIGH module, language DSP56K:LE:24:default, incomplete coverage and rough flags per its README): compiles with Ghidra 12.1.4's sleigh after one fix (slaspec line 4150: an 8-bit temp assigned to a 32-bit slice, op[24,32] -> op[24,8]); installed as a user extension (~/.config/ghidra/ghidra_12.1.4_PUBLIC/Extensions/ghidra_dsp56k, not in the Ghidra install); a headless import of the byte-swapped image (words little-endian for this spec) succeeds. Everything under work/firmware (tools/, dsp/, out/dsp_main.asm, ghproj3; git-ignored). Not done yet: naming the real P-memory base of the image, the host-port receive handler (HI08 vectors), the MIDI path on the DSP **[Later: F020-F034 are the 15 sectors of one stream, F035 a separate image; the word count included the sector header (E234, E256).]** | tool runs on the user's firmware image | work/firmware/ | HIGH (tools work), open (DSP analysis itself) |
| E233 | 2026-10-18 | The DSP's host-port receive path (TI2 DSP image; real P addresses since E234, listings work/firmware/out/dsp_P_000000.asm and dsp_X_04f000.asm; corrected the same day - the first reading had the two rings' roles swapped). HI08 receive interrupt (vector P:$60) -> handler 0x5237d: tests bit 7 of the received 24-bit word and appends the word to one of two X-memory rings with no fill check at all: bit 7 set -> ring A, write pointer X:$25, modulo 128 words; bit 7 clear -> ring B, write pointer X:$27, modulo 512 words. Which ring a word takes follows from the host side (E229/E230): the host CPU writes the interface control register with 0x39/0x31 (bit 5 = HLEND, little-endian byte order) and then three data bytes at port offsets 5, 6, 7 - single MIDI bytes as 0xFF, 0xFF, byte (0x605b for USB cables 2 and 5, the DIN UART interrupt 0x2cd9 directly), so the word is byte<<16 \| 0xFFFF with bit 7 set -> ring A; the host's own complete 3-byte messages (0x6113, 0x6270: status, data1, data2 in one word, low byte < 0x80) -> ring B. Consumers (0xd8f, called once per main-loop tick from 0x5384a, E234): ring A (0x522b7-0x522dd, read pointer X:$26) takes one word per iteration, masks the top byte (and #$ff0000) and gives it to 0x55920 (the time-stamp logic, E235), which calls the byte parser 0x522de; ring B (0x5226e-0x522a7, read pointer X:$28) saves the parser state in X:$482c1, feeds the three bytes high, middle, low to the parser 0x522a8 and restores the state before ring A continues, so a message from the host never splits a running message of the byte stream. The parser (0x522a8/0x522de): a data byte goes to the state handler in X:$482c0; a status byte 0x80-0xEF sets the handler (3-byte messages 0x52305, 2-byte 0x52310, running status via X:$482be, 0x522e9-0x52302); 0xF0-0xFF dispatch through a 16-entry table at Y:$482d1 (contents in the image: F0 0x5255b SysEx start, F1/F3 0x19b = ignore the data bytes, F2 0x540a6 song position, F4 0x5256c, F5 0x558e2 time stamp (E235), F7 0x52586 end of SysEx, F8 0x54008 clock, FA/FB/FC 0x54081/0x5408f/0x5409a start/continue/stop, FE 0x52377 active sensing (sets flag X:$482c9 and resets counter X:$482c8; the tick 0xd8f counts 750 ticks without FE and then jumps to 0x5362b, probably all notes off), F6/F9/FD/FF 0x101 = ignored). Complete channel messages: table Y:$482ca by type (8x 0x53548 note off, 9x 0x53337 note on, Ax 0x519ce, Bx 0x519ba, Cx 0x19b = program change ignored by the DSP (the host CPU loads patches), Dx 0x519d6, Ex 0x519d9), routed per part at 0x5231d-0x52366 (part table X:$48469.., per-part MIDI channel at offset 0x116, global channel Y:$48466 when Y:$48467 is set) into 0x5605b, which calls the handler for every part on that channel. Consequences for a flood: (1) ring A overruns silently - the live MIDI byte ring is only 128 bytes deep (not 512): once more than 128 bytes arrive between two ticks the write pointer laps the read pointer and the stream is corrupted (lost bytes, wrong running status, a SysEx never terminated); nothing counts or reports it, unlike the host CPU's ring reset (E230); (2) the host side has no ready check either (E229), so a word can be overwritten in the host port before the DSP's interrupt takes it; (3) the DEBUGNE at 0x52322 in the channel path tests that the modifier register m1 (copied via m0/m6) is still linear (0xFFFFFF) - an invariant on the code's own registers, not on the data, so a corrupted stream cannot reach it; all traps are assessed in E234. Not tested on hardware **[Later: ring A is drained every tick; overflow only above ~88 kB/s, not reachable through the link (E248).]** | static analysis of the user's firmware image (dsp56kDisassemble listings, E232/E234) | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm, dsp_P_000000.asm, bank00.txt | HIGH (handler, rings, consumers, parser, tables, no fill check), MED (HLEND byte order: inferred from the parser comparing bytes in the top byte and the 0xFF, 0xFF padding mapping to 'ignored'; the HI08 mode bits were not decoded) |
| E234 | 2026-10-18 | DSP image format and memory map (TI2 image, flash sectors F020-F02E, 15 x 32 KB): each sector starts with a 3-byte header (remaining sectors 0x0E..0x00, then 0x2BA9; the last sector 0x2924) followed by 10921 24-bit words and 2 padding bytes; the words of all sectors form one stream in the DSP56300 host-bootstrap format: count (0x47), start address (0x100), 71 words loaded to P:$100 - a second-stage loader that copies itself to P:$80000 and then reads records from the host port: type word (0 = P memory, 1 = X, 2 = Y, 3 = packed pairs of 12-bit values into Y, 4 = jump to the address), address, count, count data words (handlers 0x133/0x136/0x139/0x13c/0x146, table at 0x12e). The main image has 17 records: P:$0-$124b (vectors and interrupt code, 4684 words), X/Y:$16-$4f, X/Y:$50-$81, X/Y:$b0e-$b8a, X/Y:$c00-$d68, X:$1e70-$1fb6, Y:$40000-$453bd (from 0x40082 as 12-bit pairs), X:$453e0-$45c62, X:$45c80-$45cba, X:$4692d-$49240, X:$4f000-$6f8bc (133309 words - the main code, written as X but executed as P: the address attribute registers map external SRAM 0x40000-0x7FFFF for P, X and Y alike, AAR0 = 0x40639 at 0x107/0xd17), then jump to P:$d0c (reset, PLL, AARs, jmp 0x51f8f). E233's first listing counted the sector headers as words, so its offsets o >= 0x74a2 are P addresses 0x4f000 + o - 0x74a2 minus one per crossed sector (0xa823 = P:$5237d, confirmed by the HI08 receive vector at P:$60). Main-loop tick: 0x53840 calls in order 0x5f236, 0x53105, 0x53c6f, 0x5586e (stamped-event queue, E235), 0x529fd, 0xd8f (host-port rings, E233), 0x5630a, 0x5ce77, 0x54ba5, 0x55a4d, 0x548ea, 0x54149, then 0xde0; P:$101 is a bare RTS used as 'conditional return' target (jcc 0x101), P:$19b sets the parser to ignore data bytes. DEBUGcc traps: the real listing has 79 conditional DEBUGcc words; 63 are data (0x6cefd-0x6f7ad tables, 0x62127-0x6974c mixed with transfer-instruction soup, 0x5617e between 0xAAAA/0x5555 constants); 16 are code: 0x52037 (table pointer check), 0x52322 (m1 linear, E233), 0x53b19/0x53b2d (pool allocator 0x53b0f/0x53b23 walked past its (base, size) list = pool exhausted), 0x53bcf and 0x5c45d (list bound), 0x54c4c/0x54c63/0x54d2b/0x54ddd (pointer within X:$1f8c-$1f8e / 0x54d06 / 0x54dbb-c bounds), 0x54da5/0x54da8/0x54dac (overlay check: p:(r5) must read 0x000200 below 0x80000), 0x55eee/0x56457 (voice index r6 < 0xc00 after 0xb97/0xb8a), 0x55fb4 (per-part counter x:(part+4) decremented below 0 on voice release). None tests a MIDI byte; all are bounds checks on the code's own pointers, counters and pools. Whether a corrupted stream can drive one of them (the pool allocator and the per-part counter are the only data-shaped ones) is open. The rule-2 hang therefore has no proven DSP mechanism yet; the DSP's 128-byte live ring (E233) and the host's unchecked port writes remain the data-loss points **[Later: the last of the 15 sectors is F034, not F02E (E256).]** | static analysis (work/firmware/scripts/dsprecords.py, dsp56kDisassemble -pc) | work/firmware/dsp/rec/*, out/dsp_P_000000.asm, out/dsp_X_04f000.asm | HIGH (format, records, addresses, tick order), MED (trap classification by context) |
| E235 | 2026-10-18 | How the DSP handles F5 time stamps (static, same listing). The F5 status (table entry Y:$482d6 -> 0x558e2) takes two data bytes (0x558e9, 0x558f0): stamp = first byte + 128 x second byte (14 bits, low 7 bits first), compared with the DSP's stamp clock X:$55865: late (stamp < clock) -> the following bytes play immediately (X:$55866 = 0, 0x55919); stamp - clock <= threshold X:$5586c -> immediately as well; otherwise the following bytes are not parsed but queued (0x5592d: ring at X:$55863/$55864, modulo 2046 words, one byte per word; the stamp itself is queued as F5 + value, 0x55912) until the next F5 arrives at 0x55920, which ends the queued block and is evaluated afresh. The queue is played by 0x5586e-0x558cf once per tick, before the live rings (E234): while the oldest queued stamp is more than the threshold ahead of the clock the routine waits (0x55895), otherwise it feeds bytes to the parser 0x522de up to the next F5 in the queue; while the queue plays, a gate X:$5586a holds the live byte ring back unless the parser is in the middle of a message (checked in 0x5226e/0x522be). The clock X:$55865 is loaded from a word of a structure received with the audio stream (0x53cbe: x:$55247 + 11, plus 0x40000; kept unchanged while X:$48293 is set) and advanced by the block size y:$16 per tick (0x5588b-0x55892); the threshold X:$5586c is derived from X:$5413b - y:$2a, clamped to 1023 and slewed by 16 per tick (0x5586e-0x55888; 1023 while X:$5412e is 0). Only the byte stream (ring A, E233) passes 0x55920, so stamps apply to cable 2/5 bytes from the plug-in and to DIN input alike, never to the host's own 3-byte words. The F2 song-position handler 0x540a6 stores the position (Y:$53eb9) and calls 0x5311c/0x53141; the link between F2 and stamps being honoured (native/docs/protocol.md) is not visible in this path. Units of clock and stamp (sample frames of the audio stream?) and the structure read at 0x53cbe are open (E236). Not tested on hardware **[Later: X:$48293 is a strap pin written once at reset (master/slave); control words are DSP-to-DSP; master clock reset by the ping (E245, E246).]** | static analysis (dsp56kDisassemble listing) | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm | MED (stamp assembly, queue, threshold, gate), LOW (clock source and units) |
| E236 | 2026-10-18 | Where the DSP's stamp clock comes from, and what ring B carries (static, same listings). (1) Clock: the audio ring is Y:$4a000-$4bfff (r7/m7 = 0x1fff, 16 words per sample frame, 512 frames; the ESSI interrupts 0xd1f/0xd3f/0xd5e write three transmit words and store one received word per frame and advance r7 by 16). Each tick 0x53c6f tests a sync marker 0xedc987 at Y:$4a00b (the DSP writes it itself after re-aligning the frame pointer X:$17, 0x53932/0x53ea5-0x53eb1; when it is missing the tick takes 0x53e85 and writes the marker to Y:$4a005 instead). With the marker intact it writes 7 status words (clock X:$55865, X:$548d4, X:$53c59, X:$53c61-64) at column 5 of every second frame, reads 7 control words at column 11 of every second frame - word 0 plus 0x40000 becomes the stamp clock X:$55865 (unless X:$48293 is set: 0 in the image and never written by DSP code, so only the host could set it), words 1-6 go to X:$53c58/$53c5a/$53c65-68 - and then command words 0x123456+n (n = 0..5) dispatched through the table at 0x53c69. The tick advances the clock by 0x10000, and the F5 compare divides by 1024, so one tick = 64 stamp units; if the unit is one sample frame (consistent with the 14-bit sample-frame stamps measured in native/docs/protocol.md) the DSP block is 64 frames (1.45 ms at 44.1 kHz), the threshold X:$5586c (max 1023; 0x54b1e derives it from the setting X:$54a2c and y:$2a = 512, fixed 1536 while X:$54144 is set) is up to 23 ms, and the +0x40000 is a fixed lead of 256 frames. The control words arrive with the audio stream from the USB chip; the constants 0xedc987 and 0x123456 do not occur as literals in the USB chip's or host CPU's code (banks 0-6), so who composes them, and the unit, stay open. (2) Ring B (E233) is the host CPU's own command channel to the DSP: the words written by 0x6113/0x6270 (E229) are sequences 'F4 <cmd> <value>' followed by 3-byte data words (bytes < 0x80), e.g. F4 75 55 then 11 66 00, F4 F4 73 then 00 7F 40 or <x> 10 40 (callers at bank00 4851, 6435, 7460, 8873, 8962): the undefined MIDI status F4 is the private command prefix, DSP handler 0x5256c (table Y:$482d5). TI Revive never sends F4 and must not (rule 2: it would drive the DSP's parameter machinery directly). Not tested on hardware **[Later: X:$48293 is written once at reset from a strap pin (master/slave); the control words are DSP-to-DSP (E245); master clock reset by the ping (E246).]** **[Open: threshold X:$5586c given as "max 1023" and as "fixed 1536 while X:$54144 is set"; not reconciled here (E262).]** | static analysis (dsp56kDisassemble listings, Ghidra bank00.txt) | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm, dsp_P_000000.asm, bank00.txt | MED (ring layout, marker, control-word positions, tick = 64 units, F4 channel), LOW (unit = sample frame, who composes the control words) |
| E237 | 2026-10-18 | The DSP's Active Sensing watchdog, and what the plug-in did wrong with it (static, DSP listing; plug-in code). The FE handler 0x52377 sets X:$482c9 = 1 and X:$482c8 = 0; the main-loop tick 0xd8f (E234) increments X:$482c8 while X:$482c9 is set and, at 750 ticks without another FE, clears X:$482c9 (one-shot) and runs 0x5362b: 0x536e1 walks the voice list and releases every voice whose level is above 0x100000 (jsr 0x257), 0x5332b clears two 12-word blocks at X:$49b3e/$49b50 and X:$485a6 (controller/hold state), 0x543b2 resets the voice bookkeeping (X:$54137/$54138). With a 64-frame tick (E236, LOW) that is about 1.1 s at 44.1 kHz; without any FE the watchdog is never armed. The original Virus Control sends FE every 0.31 s on cable 2 (E025), so the watchdog never fires with it. TI Revive's plug-in sent exactly one FE at each timeline anchor (virusplugin.cpp, the anchored_ block, before this change) - the worst case: about a second after every anchor and re-anchor the DSP released all sounding voices once. Change 2026-10-18 (branch round-0.8.26): native/src/device/keepalive.h (ActiveSensing: one FE per 300 ms = 13230 frames on the stamped timeline, at most one per block after a timeline jump), armed at the anchor, emitted next to the F8 clock; unit test tests/test_keepalive.cpp. Built on Linux (vtitests 227/227, plug-in target VirusTINative), not yet tested on hardware | static analysis (dsp56kDisassemble listing) + plug-in source | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm, dsp_P_000000.asm; native/plugin/source/virusplugin.cpp | HIGH (handler, counter, one-shot release), MED (what 0x5362b releases), LOW (1.1 s - depends on the tick length) |
| E238 | 2026-10-18 | Bound on stamped bytes in flight, and a quantitative match with the known hang (static, E235). The DSP's stamped-event queue (X:$55863/$55864, 2046 words, one byte per word) has no fill check: everything stamped more than the threshold (<= 1023 units, E235) ahead of the DSP clock is parked there until due. With the plug-in's lead of about 7000 frames (K, native/docs/protocol.md), every stamped byte sits in that queue for about 160 ms, so more than 2046 bytes sent within 160 ms wrap the queue and corrupt the stream silently (random F5/stamp pairs, lost status bytes). The automation stress that hung the Virus (virusplugin.h: 1000 parameter changes/s plus about 2750 notes/s) is about 3750 messages/s x 6 bytes (3-byte message + 3-byte stamp) = 22 kB/s, i.e. 3.6 kB per 160 ms - well past the 2046-byte queue; the 250/s automation limit with notes on top stays far below it (1.5 kB/s + notes). This is the first mechanism in the firmware whose numbers match the observed hang threshold; whether the corrupted queue is what keeps the audio silent until a power cycle is not shown (the drain only waits up to half the 14-bit range, about 185 ms, per garbage stamp). Practical rule for TI Revive: keep stamped traffic under about 10 kB/s with bursts under 1500 bytes per 160 ms; the AutomationQueue (250/s, burst 64) already did that for parameters; since 2026-10-18 (branch round-0.8.26) all stamped traffic is bounded by a byte budget (native/src/device/sendbudget.h: 4 kB/s sustained, 1000 bytes at once, applied in the send thread before taking events and as the cap of one write; VTI_NATIVE_MIDI_RATE overrides, 0 = off; unit test tests/test_sendbudget.cpp, vtitests 230/230, plug-in builds). Not tested on hardware **[Later: the 160 ms queue residency figure is withdrawn (E261).]** | static analysis + the earlier hardware observation in virusplugin.h | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm | MED (queue size, no fill check, bound), LOW (that this is the hang) |
| E239 | 2026-10-18 | The USB-chip -> host-CPU link is lossless: a toggle handshake per byte (static; corrects E229's "a frame arriving meanwhile replaces it"). USB chip, serial interrupt 0x13e7 (instructions read with Ghidra, not only the decompiler): before every byte it XORs the level of its input T0 (P3.4) with the level it saved at the previous byte (bit 0x0b, parity trick on the accumulator) and sends only when they differ, then saves the new level; at reset it saves the inverted level so the first byte goes out at once. Host CPU, link receive interrupt 0x3288: after every received byte it toggles P1.1 (the chip's T0) - except in the two cases where it returns early without the toggle: fewer than 8 bytes free in the destination ring (frame parked, state 99, counter XDATA 0x04F7) or the DIN-out ring full (state 0x62); the main loop toggles once it has consumed the parked frame (0x14e4 region, bank00 1549/1562). So while a frame is parked the chip cannot send the next byte, its 64-byte ring fills, it NAKs the PC (E229) and the PC's transfer waits. Nothing is lost between the PC and the host CPU's rings; the pacing of SysEx dumps is done by the Virus itself through this chain (the host parser takes 8 ring bytes per main-loop pass, E230). Remaining loss points towards the synth: our broker's 1 s timeout per 32-byte transfer (E231: a timeout drops the rest of the message - with a lossless link a timeout means the Virus is really stalled for a second, e.g. busy with a patch load), the DSP's unchecked live ring (128 bytes per tick, E233) and its stamp queue (E238, now bounded). Change 2026-10-18 (branch round-0.8.26): the broker retries a timed-out packet up to 5 times in a row instead of dropping the rest of the message (src/broker/writeretry.h, used by vti_write in vticore.c (libusb) and vticore_winusb.c; the WinUSB retry continues after the bytes the pipe already took); unit test native/tests/test_writeretry.cpp (vtitests 235/235), Linux and mingw brokers build; not tested on hardware **[Later: the live ring is drained every tick; overflow only above ~88 kB/s, not reachable through the link (E248).]** | static analysis (Ghidra headless; work/firmware/scripts/DisasFunc.java for the chip's 0x13e7) | work/firmware/out/tusb_13e7.txt, tusbcode.txt, bank00.txt | HIGH (handshake on both sides), MED (T0 = P1.1 wiring is inferred from the matching toggle logic, not from a schematic) |
