| E245 | 2026-10-18 | X:$48293 is the DSP's identity, and the audio-ring control words are the other DSP's (static, DSP listings; closes E236's "who composes them"). The word is written once, in the reset code 0x51fb6-0x51fbb: bit 15 of the HI08 port's GPIO data register (M_HDR) after the port is enabled - a strap pin, never changed afterwards (the only store in the image; X and Y above 0x40000 are the same SRAM, E234). The TI2 has two DSPs running this one image; the flag selects the ESAI set-up (0x51f48 vs 0x51f4d, ESAI_1 clock bits 0x51fd9-0x51fdb) and about a dozen behaviours. The exchange in the audio ring (E236) is symmetric: each tick a DSP writes 7 status words at column 5 of every second frame (its stamp clock first) and 0x123456+n command words at column 5 of the odd frames 128 frames ahead (pointer X:$53c55, writers 0x53d31/0x53d4b/0x53d68/0x53dd7/0x53de2/0x53dfb), and reads the same layout at column 11 (status) and 27 (commands, dispatch table 0x53c69, 6 entries). The received word 0 + 0x40000 becomes the clock only on the DSP whose flag is clear (0x53cb8-0x53cbe); the DSP with the flag set keeps its own clock. So the master DSP owns the stamp clock, the slave takes it from the master's status word with a fixed lead of 256 frames (the link's round trip through the ring, 0x53d73 subtracts the same 256 << 10 from a received command payload), and the constants 0xedc987 (marker, written by the DSP at 0x53934/0x53e85/0x53ead) and 0x123456 are DSP-to-DSP - they are not in the USB chip's or host CPU's code because those never see them. Command 0x12345b carries a channel message (status, data1, data2: sender 0x53df8 from the channel-message handlers via 0x52cab, receiver 0x53e07 -> 0x52cad): the master forwards MIDI channel messages to the slave through the audio ring; the slave's notes are therefore at the master's timing plus the link. The unit: both clocks advance by the block (y:$16 = 64 frames) per tick on the DSP's own frame clock and the master's is reset by the sync ping (E246), nothing outside the DSPs feeds it - one stamp unit is one sample frame at the Virus' rate (consistent with the frame-exact measurement, protocol.md). Which ESAI carries the link is not pinned down: the ESAI handlers at P:$d1f/$d3f/$d5e use columns 0, 2, 3, 6, 7, 9, 12-14 and the ESAI_1 fast interrupts (P:$70/$78) two words at r7 each, so columns 4/5 and 10/11 belong to ESAI_1 (MED) | DSP listing: 0x51fb6, 0x53c6f-0x53eb1, 0x53df8, P:$d1f-$d6f, P:$70-$79 | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm, dsp_P_000000.asm | HIGH (identity flag, symmetric exchange, master/slave clock, 0x12345b forwarding), HIGH (unit = sample frame), MED (ESAI_1) |
| E246 | 2026-10-18 | The stamp clock's life: who resets it, how stamps are compared, and that nothing outside the DSP moves it (static, DSP listing; closes "X:$48293 clock freeze" and bears on E243). The sync ping (`F0 00 20 33 01 dev 73 00 22 vv F7`, protocol.md) is parsed by the DSP itself (SysEx handler 0x5255b; the page-0x73 parameter table at Y:$48054, two words per parameter: 0x22 -> 0x55948, 0x23 -> 0x540ca, 0x1a-0x1e -> 0x5197f/0x50eec/0x50ef0/0x52226/0x5211a, most others the ignore handler 0x519b0). Handler 0x55949: value < 0x7E -> clock X:$55865 = 0 and the current stamp X:$55868 = 0 on BOTH DSPs; the slave (flag clear, E245) also renders the marker: it loads a 7-word voice template (0x18e) and runs the generator 0x55395 with the table 0x5593c once (the single sample on USB 2 that the plug-in finds, protocol.md), then restores template 6. Value 0x7E installs a per-tick hook (X:$5595e = 0x5596a, called from the stamp-queue tick 0x5588a) that keeps rendering through 0x505dc into the 64-frame output buffers 0x4c200/0x4c240 (a continuous test signal, slave only); 0x7F removes the hook. The compare (0x558f0-0x5591f): stamp - clock is taken as a signed 24-bit difference of frames << 10, so the window is modular: a stamp up to 8191 frames ahead of the clock is "future" (queued when more than the threshold ahead, else played at once), one up to 8192 behind is "late" (played at once, clock - 1 becomes the current stamp, no report to the host). Only three things write the clock: the reset above, the tick advance (0x5588b-0x55892, + block), and on the slave the master's status word (0x53cbe). No host-CPU command, no USB event and no audio-stream condition touches it. Consequence for E243/E244: the Virus' clock cannot have "moved" by itself during a Mac export - the plug-in's ring lost frames (E249) **[Later: the K drop 6024 -> 512 is probably the 5512-frame matching artifact (E283); the export fault was block size / OfflinePacer (E315).]** **[Open: page-0x73 parameter table given as Y:$48054 here but as $48053 in E255; not checked which is right (E255).]** | DSP listing 0x55949-0x55984, 0x558f0-0x5591f, Y:$48054-$48140 | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm, dsp_X_04692d.asm | HIGH (reset path, modular compare, writers of the clock), MED (0x7E = test signal: the generator was not followed into the audio) |
| E247 | 2026-10-18 | F2 (song position) has no part in the DSP's stamp handling; the old "without F2 the stamps are ignored" (protocol.md, 2026-10-13) is explained by E246's modular window (static). The F2 handler 0x540a6-0x540c8 stores the position as 24-ppqn ticks (position in sixteenths x 6 - 1 -> Y:$53eb9), sets Y:$53eba = 0xFF, and calls 0x5311c (copies the tick accumulator Y:$53eb7/8 and the tempo values Y:$53ec0/1 to the clock-synced modulation state X/Y:$b54-$b58) and 0x53141 (arpeggiator/LFO phase Y:$48573) - the DSP's internal clock sync (arp, LFO, delay), nothing near the stamp clock, the threshold or the queue. FA/FB/FC do the same with the position reset (0x54081, 0x5408f, 0x5409a). The DSP evaluates the stamped F8 ticks for its tempo (0x54008 -> 0x5404c: (current stamp - clock) >> 10). Why the 2026-10-13 experiments correlated with F2: before the marker was known the stamps were ring-relative, so the Virus' clock sat at a random offset per session; a send-ahead of 2000-5000 frames is "future" (honoured) only when the offset leaves it inside the 8191-frame window, about 44 % of sessions, and "late" otherwise - the honoured/immediate runs were session luck, not F2 (3 honoured runs with F2, 1/3 without, protocol.md). With the marker the window question is settled (stamps are clock-relative) and F2/FE at the anchor remain useful only for the Virus' own tempo sync and the watchdog (E237) | DSP listing 0x540a6-0x540c8, 0x5311c-0x5317a, 0x54008-0x54080 | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm | HIGH (what F2 touches), MED (the old observation's explanation) |
| E248 | 2026-10-18 | Rule-2 hang on the DSP side, reassessed (static). (1) The live byte ring A (128 words, E233) is drained completely every tick (0x522be loop, no per-tick cap) and only held while queued stamped bytes play (gate X:$5586a, cleared when the queue runs dry or the parser is between messages); it overflows only above ~88 kB/s within one 1.45 ms tick - unreachable through the USB chip and host CPU (E229/E231), so a flood does not corrupt it. (2) DEBUGcc traps halt the core: on a DSP56300 the instruction enters OnCE debug mode and waits for a debugger, i.e. the DSP stops producing audio until a reset; the host CPU writes the host port without a ready check (E230), so it then blocks too - the whole Virus freezes, panel included (E241's symptom). The 16 trap sites (E234) are reachable from MIDI only indirectly: the pool allocator 0x53b0f (first-fit over a free list X:$53b03/$53b04, total X:$53b06; trap 0x53b19 when no block fits) is used by the part/patch set-up (0x56006 and 18 more sites allocate voice/effect structures with templates from X:$45c80; the only frees are the teardown 0x551d8-0x55203), not per note - a flood does not drain it; the per-part counter trap 0x55fb4 guards a count the code itself decrements. (3) The stamped-event queue (2046 bytes, no fill check, E238) remains the only MIDI-driven corruption: once it wraps, the parser 0x522de receives arbitrary bytes - including 0xF4, which selects the host CPU's private command handler 0x5256c (sub-commands by data byte: part selection 0x525e0, mode switches 0x525ce/0x525d3, state machine Y:$482e4; E236) - so a wrapped queue can issue random host commands to the synth engine; a halt through one of the traps or a mis-set state is then plausible but not proven. The practical rule stands: bound the stamped traffic (sendbudget.h) and never send F4 or the Multi "Original Patch" 72 00 03 (page 0x72 param 3 is handled by the host CPU's multi parser, not analysed) **[Later: "the host CPU blocks too" is wrong: the host-port writes never wait, a halted DSP just loses the bytes (E262).]** **[Open: simulator S100 sees about 100 clone/free pairs per session through 0x53beb, against 'only frees are the teardown'; maybe two heaps (0x53b0f vs 0x53b71), unresolved.]** | DSP listing 0x522be-0x522dd, 0x53b0f-0x53b7e, 0x55fa6-0x55fb8, 0x5256c-0x5262d | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm | HIGH (ring drain, trap semantics, allocator use), MED (queue wrap -> F4 path), LOW (which trap halts) |
| E249 | 2026-10-18 | Mechanism for the Mac export clock shift (E243/E244), from the firmware reading (E246: the Virus' clock has no external input) plus our code: on macOS the plug-in's audio ring is filled by a Core Audio IOProc (native/src/device/audioin.cpp io_proc) that appends every buffer it gets and ignores the input time stamp. Core Audio skips IO cycles when the system is overloaded (a Live export renders at full CPU and the IOProc runs in Live's process) and reports them only through the next cycle's inInputTime.mSampleTime; each skipped cycle shortens the ring by the device's buffer size without a trace, while the Virus' clock runs on. The plug-in stamps events as ring frame + lead - marker, so after a skipped 5600 frames (eleven 512-frame cycles) every stamp lands 5600 frames behind the Virus' clock: late, played on arrival, "sync errors" until the next power cycle or marker - the K drop from ~6100 to ~511 in E243 and the same in 0.8.25 (E244: the IOProc code is older than the paced render). Linux does not show it because the broker's libusb reader fills USB gaps with silence against the wall clock (vtiusbaudio_libusb.c, "stream stalled, N ms filled with silence"; the Linux journal shows these at every wake, 19:21:56 and 19:35:07-11 on 2026-10-18, never during a render). Fix (branch round-0.8.27, not on hardware yet): native/src/device/gapfill.h follows the device's sample time - the frames between the ring's length and sample_time - origin become silence before the buffer (gaps up to 5 s; a backwards or longer jump re-bases, the StallWatch handles sleep); io_proc applies it when kAudioTimeStampSampleTimeValid is set; "filled N" is new in the plug-in's stats line. Unit tests tests/test_gapfill.cpp (in step, 10 skipped cycles -> 5120 frames, fractional times, device restart, 19 s sleep); vtitests 242/242 and the plug-in build on Linux; the macOS part compiles only there (CI). Hardware check: Live export on the Mac, then klog K before and after the export equal, "filled" > 0 during the export, no sync error page. Residual on Linux/macOS broker streams (LOW, not seen): the broker's filler acts only when 30 ms or more are missing, so up to 30 ms of ring slip can persist **[Later: the K drop 6024 -> 512 is probably the 5512-frame matching artifact (E283); the export fault was block size / OfflinePacer (E315).]** | audioin.cpp io_proc (before this change), E243/E244 logs, Linux broker journal | native/src/device/audioin.cpp, gapfill.h, tests/test_gapfill.cpp; journalctl --user -u tirevive-broker.service | MED (mechanism: fits every observation, not yet confirmed by a "filled" count on the Mac), HIGH (the Virus side is not the cause, E246) |
| E250 | 2026-10-18 | What the DSP tells the host CPU, and the origin of the Sync Information Event (73 00 54 01) is still open (static). DSP -> host CPU goes through the HI08 transmit ring (640 words at X:$4c800, write X:$29 / read X:$2a; 0x523b9 queues one byte and enables HTIE, the ISR 0x52396 drains it; the timer-0 ISR P:$da1 exchanges one word each way per tick as well). Senders found: (a) 0x531bb, run every tick from 0x53105, recomputes a beat-phase flag X:$48572 from the tick accumulator and, when it changes, sends 'F5 <0\|1>' (plus a part update 0x56189 on 1) - the tempo indicator, not an error; (b) 0x53180 sends a status word with 'F8 00 00' when the clock-synced state Y:$b56 crosses zero; (c) 0x5503f composes a SysEx-shaped block 'F0 00 20 33 00 00 0B 00 <5 bytes> F7' in 4-byte link frames (header word = X:$480c9 << 4 \| 0x40000); (d) 0x529cd/0x5254x send parameter dumps framed by 0xF4 0x00 0xF4. None carries 0x54. On the host CPU the SysEx builder for the pages 0x6e-0x74 (FUN_CODE_654a: F0 00 20 33 01 dev page param value F7) is called only with page 0x74 (FUN_CODE_085e), and FUN_CODE_6511 (same pages) only with 0x73 and parameters 0x00/0x01/0x55/0x7f; the 0x54 report is composed elsewhere (a table-driven sender, or the value 0x54 is computed) - open. Working hypothesis (LOW): the host CPU reports it when the DSP's beat flag (a) toggles while the stamped F8 ticks are late (E243: ~3 reports/s with every FE/F8 late) **[Later: FUN_6511 is called for pages 0x6e-0x73 from FUN_1102 (E252); the 0x5503f sender is unreachable (E260).]** | DSP listing 0x523b9, 0x52396, 0x531bb, 0x53180, 0x5503f, 0x529cd; host bank00 FUN_CODE_654a/6511/085e | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm, bank00.txt, bank06.txt | HIGH (the channels and senders), open (0x54) |
| E251 | 2026-10-18 | Origin of the Sync Information Event `F0 00 20 33 01 10 73 00 54 01 F7` (closes E250's open point; static, DSP + host CPU listings). The DSP composes it itself: the MIDI-clock handler (F8, 0x54008 -> 0x5404c) compares the newest received time stamp (X:$55868, kept at max(newest stamp, clock - 1) by 0x558cf) with its stamp clock; while the stamp is ahead the tick is "on time" (0x54056-0x54074, which also re-arms the continuation Y:$53ecf = 0x54075); the first tick after that whose newest stamp is behind the clock runs 0x54075: it stores the tick time, sets the continuation to 0x54011 (further late ticks are silent) and calls 0x5411f, which fills page 0x73, part 0 (0x40 when Y:$48467 is set; it is 0), parameter 0x54, value 1 and jumps to the DSP's SysEx sender 0x52484. That routine writes the whole message as USB-MIDI packets into the host-port ring (0x523b9): a header word per packet = cable X:$480cb (= 2 in the data record) << 4 \| CIN 4, the last packet CIN 6, bytes F0 00 20 / 33 01 <Y:$482ed = 0x10> / 73 00 54 / 01 F7. On the host CPU the INT0 interrupt (vector 0x0003 -> FUN_2e80 -> FUN_2644; INT0 = the DSP's host request) reads the first byte: 0xF5 = beat flag (bit _7_2, the LED pattern FUN_5c63/FUN_5c05 with global 0x32), 0xF4 = a DSP parameter report (page, param, value -> a parameter record with page \| 0x80), anything else = a 4-byte USB-MIDI frame copied unchanged into the USB transmit queue 0x6C00-0x6FFF (columns 0x6C-0x6F) while the plug-in link is up (_4_4), so the DSP's SysEx reaches the PC on cable 2 of EP 0x86 exactly as captured. Meaning: the Virus reports once each time its newest stamp falls behind its clock at a clock tick after having been ahead - "the stamps have caught up with real time", one event per transition; a steady stream of late stamps gives one event, a lead that hovers around zero gives several per second (E074: 15/s, E243: 3-6/s with the stamped FE every 300 ms moving X:$55868). Late stamps themselves still play (E246); the event is the symptom of a lead margin that is too small, not of a lost note. Other DSP reports use the same sender: 0x52453 turns page-0x70 changes into CCs (status 0xB0) and page-0x71 into 0xA0 messages while X:$4810f / X:$48111 are set (both 1), everything else into SysEx; 0x5503f sends an internal message `F0 00 20 33 00 00 0B 00 <5 bytes> F7` on cable X:$480c9 (= 1, the MIDI port EP 0x85; meaning open) **[Later: the part byte of 73 xx 54 01 varies per session, 0x40 in 505 real cases (E429).]** | DSP listing 0x54008-0x54080, 0x5411f-0x5412b, 0x52484-0x524cf, 0x52453; data X:$480cb/$480c9, Y:$482ed/$48467; host bank00 FUN_2e80, FUN_2644 (asm via DisasFuncs.java), FUN_5c63 | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm, dsp/rec/X_04692d.bin, f_bank00*.txt | HIGH |

| E257 | 2026-10-18 | What the host CPU's 0x4F frames are, and that the USB chip owns the sample rate (closes E252's open point; static, USB chip asm via DisasAt.java on tusbcode.bin + host asm). The USB chip's main loop reads a completed 4-byte frame from the host CPU (INTMEM 0x9e-0xa1, serial ISR 0x13e7) and routes it by the cable nibble: cable 0/1 -> the EP 0x85 IN ring, every other cable -> the EP 0x86 IN ring (the plug-in interface), except cable 4 (0x4x), which is a message to the chip itself: the next byte is a key dispatched through the table at code 0x0616 (FUN_098c), the byte after it the value. Keys are page-0x73 global parameters and two commands: 0x0F "System Clock/SA Freq" -> INTMEM 0x86 = value, 0x87 = 3 (staged); 0x10 "System Clock/Frequency" -> value 0: rate 44100 (0xAC44), 1: rate 48000 (0xBB80), compared with the current rate (0x10b0) and, when different, stored in INTMEM 0x7e/0x7f with the change flag bit 0x07 - the chip switches its audio clock; 0x11 "System Clock/Mode" -> INTMEM 0x84 (0 or 1); 0x1F "Analog Input/Gain" -> INTMEM 0x80 = value & 3; 0x2B "SPDIF/Input Routing" -> INTMEM 0x82 (0 or 1); 0xF5 value 0/1 -> clears/sets bit 0x11 (link mode); 0xF6 -> bytes into a 16-byte buffer (0x9c) then P3.5 (a transfer to the chip's peripheral, open); unknown keys -> bit 0x0e. The host CPU sends such a frame whenever a page-0x73 parameter changes (FUN_1102 -> FUN_27f6, from the panel, the DSP or the PC's SysEx), and at a USB connect it re-sends 0x0F, 0x11, 0x2B and 0x1F (FUN_337a -> FUN_5bf9) - NOT 0x10. Consequence: the Virus' audio sample rate has two masters, the PC's UAC SET_CUR on the endpoints (E241) and the global parameter 0x10 "System Clock/Frequency" (0 = 44.1 kHz, 1 = 48 kHz, the panel's setting); `F0 00 20 33 01 10 73 00 10 00 F7` is the documented way to put the chip to 44.1 kHz from the MIDI side, exactly what the panel does. TI Revive sends neither 0x0F nor 0x10 today (only 0x2D at start and the ping 0x22); the original's start-up globals in our captures are 0x3A/0x3B/0x3C/0x52 (E039). Proposal for the 48 kHz state (E175, E241): when 48 kHz packets appear, send 73 00 10 00 (and read it back with the global request) in addition to SET_CUR - a normal global parameter, no vendor request, rule 2 safe; needs a code change with tests and a hardware check **[Later: 0x10b0 is a compare routine, not a variable; 73 00 10 00 changes nothing when the stored rate is equal (E331).]** **[Later: TI Revive itself sent 73 00 10 00 from the Live project's saved state; fixed (E445, E446, E447).]** | tusbcode.bin asm 0x0610-0x06f0 (f_tusb_keys.txt), tusbcode.txt 1340-1430; host bank00 FUN_27f6, FUN_337a, FUN_5bf9; XML bank F names 15/16/17/31/43 | work/firmware/out/f_tusb_keys.txt, images/tusbcode.bin | HIGH (routing, keys, rate values), MED (0xF5/0xF6 meaning) |

| E259 | 2026-10-18 | How the Access updater flashes the sectors (closes E256's open point; REA/Ghidra on the MSI custom action CAInstallerHelperDeferred, x86-64, sha256 5452ea54...). FUN_18000f3e0 loads firmware.bin, reads the TABL names and hands vti.bin / vti_snow.bin / vti_2.bin (also accepted: vti2.bin) to FUN_18000fec0 with the model index 0/1/2. That parser IGNORES the 3-byte record addresses: for every sector chunk of 0x8000, 0x8c00 or 0x8c02 bytes it takes the flash bank from the chunk TAG - 'F' and 'P' tags give 0x80 + the decimal number, 'S' tags the number itself (F020 -> bank 0x94, F034 -> 0xA2, F035 -> 0xA3, F060/F061 -> 0xBC/0xBD, P064 -> 0xC0, P120 -> 0xF8, S002 -> 0x02) - strips the addresses (copies the two 32-byte halves of each 70-byte record into a 32 KB bank buffer) and files the bank in a per-model map; a tag with any other first letter fails the parse. The device's flash page is (bank + 0x80) mod 256, which reproduces every address in E228 (F020 -> 0x14, S002 -> 0x82, P120 -> 0x78). The update procedure (FUN_180005ad0: scan the device, compare the firmware version/build with the package, update the Device Information Block = bank 0x80 with 0x60 bytes of device data merged at its end, "Writing firmware" = FUN_180007230 over banks 0x80-0xBD in pairs: erase, write 32 KB at bank*0x8000 (device bank addressing), CRC check per bank; banks 0xC0-0xFD are skipped when their CRC is already intact; then "Writing ROM Banks" FUN_180007900, then mark complete). Consequences: F035 is flashed to page 0x23 (0x230000) although its records say 0x220000 - the flat image vti2_flash.bin (E228's script) placed it wrongly; F060 and F061 are two copies of the USB chip firmware at 0x3C0000 and 0x3D0000 (redundancy, the host picks one - not followed); the "skipped since CRC is intact" rule means the ROM banks are rewritten only when damaged. No host-CPU code selects page 0x23 (no constant page select 0x23, the DSP boot stream stops at F034's remaining count 0, no third host port: XDATA 0x0205 has one unrelated use), so the 2009 "fvd102.lod" data image is flashed but, as far as the static reading goes, unused by OS 5.1.7 on the TI2 - a legacy sector | REA ev_4539a2a1..., ev_4cd5f250..., ev_9a24d324... | E261 | 2026-10-19 | Stamp threshold semantics, residency per platform and the two failure modes (static, DSP listing + the plug-in's send path; corrects E238 and E248). (1) Semantics: a stamped block whose stamp is at most the threshold X:$5586c ahead of the DSP clock is parsed at once, the stamp becomes the current stamp (0x55904) and the note-on handler 0x53337 copies it into the voice's start slot X:$54145 - the voice starts AT the stamp, so the threshold is the maximum per-voice scheduling delay, not "play now". Beyond the threshold the bytes up to the next F5 are parked in the 2046-byte queue (E235). The threshold is 1023 while the counter X:$5412e is 0 (0x55882-0x55888: teq with #$3ff), otherwise a value slewed by 16 per tick towards min(X:$5413b - y:$2a, 1023) (E262: the counter is live part set-up records, so the window only shrinks during a patch change). (2) Residency: a stamp leaves the plug-in A frames ahead of the newest ring frame (timeline + lead - marker, lead = S + A, protocol.md) and needs 512-768 frames to reach the DSP (A = 768 still exact, 512 partly late). Linux/macOS, A = 1024: the stamp is ~256-512 frames ahead when the DSP reads it - within the threshold, nothing is parked, the queue stays empty. Windows, A = 3072: ~2300-2560 ahead, ~1300-1550 frames = 30 ms parked. E238's "every stamped byte sits in the queue for about 160 ms" (from the lead K ~7000) is withdrawn: most of the lead is spent in the ring and the USB path before the DSP sees the stamp. The byte budget (sendbudget.h, 4 kB/s, burst 1000) stays as the safe bound for what the DSP can park within that 30 ms window (< 2046 bytes). E238's "quantitative match" with the 2026-10-13 stress hang is withdrawn too: that hang was on Linux with A = 1024 (nothing parked) and the Virus still answered MIDI - its mechanism is open again. (3) Gaps found in the plug-in and closed in round 0.8.27: unstamped messages on cable 2 (parameter SysEx from the editor, part dumps on a patch change, the state, CC resets) follow the previous stamp and are parked with it on Windows (two patch dumps plus a burst reach the 2046 wrap) and were not counted by the budget -> DeviceOut counts queued cable-2 bytes as a debt capped at one burst (SendBudget::take_queued: a state send of 100 kB delays stamped traffic by 250 ms, not 25 s); automation from process() inherited the previous event's stamp (on Windows up to ~1400 frames off its block time) -> its parameter SysEx gets its own F5 at the block time (stamprule.h; queued messages keep inheriting, a stamp behind the newest one would move X:$55868 and can raise the sync event, E251); no explicit guard against 72 00 03 -> sendguard.h drops page 0x72 parameters 0-3 (Flags, Original Bank, Original Patch; 4-13 = the multi name pass) and status F4 with a log line; the Windows streamer lacked E241's handling -> vtiusbaudio.c sends silent same-size OUT packets while 48 kHz packets arrive and repeats SET_CUR 3x; E257's lever (global 73 00 10 00) is sent by both brokers next to the SET_CUR retry (ratelever.h) and the Virus' own report of the global is logged when it arrives (a read-back request is not in our evidence, open). Tests: vtitests 251/251 (test_sendbudget, test_stamprule, test_sendguard, test_pktstat); plug-in, Linux broker, mingw broker and the classic wrapper build. (4) Two failure modes, to be kept apart: (a) the stress hang (2026-10-13, Linux): audio silent, MIDI and panel still answering - not the stamped queue (nothing parked), cause open; (b) E241 (Linux, after a wake): a loud signal, then the panel dead - the 48 kHz state with a starved output; E248's chain "DSP halts -> host CPU blocks on the host port -> panel dead" is withdrawn (E262: the host writes the port without any ready check and cannot block there), so a dead panel means the host CPU itself stopped, cause open. | E262 | 2026-10-19 | What the DSP's counter X:$5412e counts, where the threshold's input comes from, and that the host CPU writes the host port without a ready check (static; closes round 0.8.27 T7/T8). (1) X:$5412e is reset to 0 in the init 0x5435a (with a dozen neighbours $5412f, $54131-$54134, $54141-$54144; called from the set-up chain 0x53871 -> 0x53881), incremented once at 0x544b3 at the end of 0x54484 - which allocates a record of 0x15 words from the template at X:$1f38 (0x5419e, 0x5442f -> allocator 0x53b71, record address in X:$54135), links it into the list X:$1f43/X:$1f44, gives it a lifetime X:$1f86 = y:$17 and a remaining count X:$1f87 = -1 (= not armed yet) and bumps X:$1f3c/X:$1f3d - and decremented at 0x5457d when a record is retired: from the per-tick walk 0x5443d over the X:$1f3e live records (0x54481, when the record's count X:$1f87 is below X:$543ce) and from the teardown loop 0x54540 that retires every record (0x54544). 0x54484 is called from 0x547f8 (a loop of (X:$55c5c >> 16) + 1 records) and from 0x55fe5, the part set-up path next to the counter trap 0x55fb4 (E248). So X:$5412e = the number of live set-up records: allocated in a burst by the part/patch set-up, retired per tick as their counts run out. The threshold is 1023 only while none is alive: the DSP shortens its scheduling window during a patch change (what one record drives - a voice ramp, a crossfade segment - is not pinned down, LOW). (2) The threshold's input X:$5413b (0x54b1e): 0x600 = 1536 while the flag X:$54144 is set (0x54adb sets it to 0xff, 0x54ae2 counts it down per tick, 0x54af7 re-derives it from y:$2a), else max(2 * y:$2a, 0xc0 = 192) - the mac terms 0x54b28-0x54b2a multiply small integers in fractional mode and reach only the low accumulator word, so they do not change the stored word (MED). The setting X:$54a2c is a value <= 0x40 from X:$548d4 (0x54938-0x54953: a sum of three counters capped at 0x30, else 0x40), applied by 0x54a39 through three piecewise tables at 0x54a7b/0x54a8f/0x54aa6 (rows of threshold, value, handler = 0x54ab6 or 0x56268) and 0x54b1e; y:$2b/y:$2a come from the same setting (0x54b0c/0x54ac1: y:$2b = f(setting), y:$2a = 2 * y:$2b; cleared at 0x54ad3) and are advanced per tick by 0x54af7 (y:$2b += the block, y:$2a halved). With the threshold routine 0x5586e (min(X:$5413b - y:$2a, 1023), slewed by 16 per tick, 1023 while X:$5412e == 0; E261) the practical reading: outside a part set-up the DSP accepts stamps up to 1023 frames ahead as "now, start the voice at the stamp"; during one the window shrinks to a few hundred frames or less, so more of our Windows lead is parked and nothing changes on Linux/macOS (A = 1024, E261). (3) Host port: FUN_605b (a single MIDI byte: ICR 0x39, then 0xFF, 0xFF, byte at the data registers; callers FUN_1ffb x11, FUN_286d x4, FUN_28e7 x2, FUN_2e00) and FUN_6113 / FUN_6270 (a complete 3-byte message, ICR 0x31; callers FUN_2d84, FUN_337a, FUN_63bf / FUN_4ee8, FUN_5953, FUN_6511) are plain stores to XDATA 0x0000-0x0007 AND 0x0100-0x0107 with interrupts disabled for the duration (EA saved and cleared): no status read, no polling loop, no wait. Every word goes to both host ports, i.e. both DSPs (consistent with E245's one image on two chips; which DSP acts on a byte is decided on the DSP side - open). So E248's "the host CPU blocks on the port when the DSP halts" is wrong: the HI08 has no handshake for data writes, the 8051's bus write always completes, a halted DSP loses the bytes and the host CPU, its panel and MIDI thru keep running. E230/E233 stand. | E263 | 2026-10-19 | How the two DSPs share the MIDI work (static, DSP listing; closes E262's open point "which DSP acts on a byte"). Both DSPs receive every byte the host CPU writes (E262: both host ports) and both parse the stream: the sync ping's handler resets X:$55865 and X:$55868 on both before it tests the identity flag (0x5594c-0x55954; only the DSP with the flag clear goes on to render the marker, 0x55955, and the same test gates 0x5596a) - consistent with E245/E246. The voice handlers then execute a message only on one DSP: the note-on handler 0x53337 (0x53352-0x5335a), the handler 0x5346b (reached from the dispatcher 0x521f4) and 0x5ee8a each take the current slot index X:$54136, mask bit 6 (y:$16 = 0x40) and compare it with X:$48293 - a note runs on the master when its slot's bit 6 is clear, on the slave when set; the other DSP returns. The hand-over goes through the audio ring (E245): the sender 0x53d2c-0x53d3c writes command 0x123456 with the slot index X:$54136, the two message words (y1, y0) and the voice start stamp X:$54145 (= the note's time stamp, E261), then allocates a set-up record (0x5419e); the receiver 0x53d51 (first entry of the dispatch table 0x53c69) stores the slot index into X:$54136, looks the slot's record up (0x53d5a, field +2), reads the two words and the stamp, subtracts 0x100 << 10 (256 frames in the clock's format, 0x53d70-0x53d75) into its own X:$54145 and allocates records through 0x547e5 (E262's counter X:$5412e thus also grows on the slave from the master's commands). Slot indices: the set-up loops assign them from 0 (0x5437e, incremented per slot at 0x543ab) and from 0x10 for the 16 parts (0x562b2); the dispatcher 0x54f36/0x54f3c sets X:$54136 per incoming message. Which slots carry bit 6 (i.e. which parts or voices live on the slave) is not pinned down (MED), nor whether the slave discards its own copy of a stream note through the same bit test or never parses it (LOW). Timing reading for T9 (prediction, LOW): the slave's clock runs 256 frames ahead of the master's status word (E245), the forwarded start stamp is reduced by 256, and the slave's audio reaches the master's mix through the ring - if these cancel, a note on a slave part starts at the same output frame as on a master part; a measured ~256-frame offset between two parts would show the split instead. | DSP listing 0x5594c-0x5596e, 0x53352-0x5335a, 0x5346b-0x53475, 0x5ee8a-0x5ee93, 0x53d2c-0x53d3c, 0x53d51-0x53d7d, 0x53c69, 0x5437e-0x543b1, 0x562b2-0x562bd, 0x54f36-0x54f41 | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm | HIGH (bit-6 gate in three handlers, command layout, both clocks reset by the ping), MED (slot numbering), LOW (what runs on the slave, the timing prediction) |

Cause in the new code: while a dump went out in pieces the worker skipped its regular work (idle work, audio scan, stall watch, presence) for each round; a state holds 16 dumps = ~1.8 s of pieces, so StallWatch (LIMIT_MS 1500) saw the stream move after more than 1.5 s without a check and reported a sleep, and each resent state started the next ~1.8 s of dumps. Fix: the worker only picks the due piece in the selection and writes it after the regular work of the round. Table entries: page 0x72 parameter 0 -> 0x519b0 (the ignore handler, as E255), global 0x73/0x23 -> 0x540ca, page 0x6F parameter 0x7F -> 0x565a8. F2 (song position) is parsed by 0x540a6/0x540ad/0x540b4: the two data bytes give the position, converted to MIDI clock ticks (x 6) minus 1, stored in Y:$53eb9, then 0x5311c/0x53141 re-sync; global 0x23 (0x540ca) adds its value to Y:$53eb9 - so the original's `73 00 23 01` after F2 puts the clock exactly on the song position (the -1 undone). 0x565a8 (6F <part> 7F <v>) runs on one DSP only (0x21e tests Y:$481a6), takes the part's voice slot (0x24c -> X:$54136, E263) and stores v x 0x6400 in a per-part word; the original always sends v = 0; what that word controls: E276 (a hidden Atomizer/input value per part, MED). The original sends F2 + 73 00 23 01 (device id 00) + 16 x 6F <part> 7F 00 in one write at every Play and loop restart (captures, E274). TI Revive sent F2 only when it anchored its timeline - the arpeggiator and clock-synced LFOs did not restart with the bar. Change: transport.h (TransportWatch: Play or a jump of the host position by more than 0.01 quarter; transport_messages()), sent from process() at the block's time; nativehost --transport T --loop N for tests. Device check (nativehost --transport 10 --loop 2, usbmon capture captures/ours-transport-1): the set goes out at Play and every 4 s (2 bars at 120 BPM) in the original's order, notes audible throughout; vtitests 261/261. Hardware effect on an arpeggio not heard yet **[Later: 6F pp 7F vv is the per-part analog output delay in ms, now sent by TI Revive, outputdelay.h (E344).]** | DSP static analysis, captures of the original and of TI Revive | work/firmware/out (local), captures/ours-transport-1.pcapng (local) | HIGH (F2 + 0x23 semantics, the set on the wire), LOW (6F/7F's meaning) |
| E276 | 2026-10-19 | What page D parameter 127 (`6F <part> 7F <v>`, sent by the original with v = 0 at every Play and loop restart, E275) belongs to (static, DSP code record X_04f000, data record X_04692d). Page D 124-126 are the input block (Input/Mode, Input/Select, Input/Atomizer; Access parameter list), 127 is hidden right after it. The Atomizer handler (6F/7E -> 0x5ee81) gates on the part's voice slot like E263 (X:$54136 bit 6 against X:$48293), keeps the part's Atomizer setting in the per-part word X:(r2+$566b0) and, when it switches between zero and non-zero, rebuilds the part's processing (0x21e, 0x541f6). The handler of 127 (0x565a8) writes the NEXT word of the same per-part record, X:(r2+$566b1), and the word X:$565a7 (default 0x6400), both as v x 0x6400 << 7; both addresses are operands of a compiled processing chain at 0x56570-0x565a7 whose routines include 0x5c344, which writes incoming samples into a circular Y-memory buffer (modulo addressing) - the shape of the Atomizer's input buffer. Reading: parameter 127 is a hidden Atomizer/input value per part that the original sets to 0 at every transport start (a reset of the Atomizer's buffer or sync at Play, MED for "Atomizer", LOW for the exact effect). It matters only for parts that process the input (Input Mode on, Atomizer); TI Revive sends it like the original since E275 **[Later: 6F pp 7F vv is the per-part analog output delay in ms, now sent by TI Revive, outputdelay.h (E344).]** | DSP static analysis (dsp56kDisassemble -nops at 0x5ee81, 0x565a8, 0x5c344; pointer search over both records) | work/firmware/out, work/firmware/dsp/rec (local) | MED (Atomizer relation), LOW (exact effect) |

| E279 | 2026-10-19 | Late start after a render at 48 kHz (E278, user's L3 note) reproduced and fixed. Headless reproduction on the TI2 (nativehost --rate 48000 --block 1024 --notes --transport 2 --offline 10 --offline-end 18 --ahead 300@22, new option --offline-end): note latency steady at 61.4 ms during the render; after it the plug-in re-anchored 15 times in 10 s (the 'jump back to real time' branch for \|err\| > 512) and the notes came 55.0, 56.4, 58.2 ... 60.9 ms - about 8 s to settle. Cause: a timeline jump moves the direct 44.1 kHz read position at once (read_at follows the target), but the resampled path (host rate != 44.1 kHz) only steers its read position towards the target at <= 0.2 % speed unless the error exceeds 2048 frames; REAPER's ~1000-frame jump after the render took >10 s (the takes' +1800 ppm, E278). Fix: rs_valid_ = false on every anchor and every jump back to real time, so the resampled output jumps with the timeline like the direct one. After the fix, same run: 61.1 ms from the first note after the render on (61.0-61.2 ms to the end); the one note exactly at the switch (18 s) came 17 ms late (in a DAW the transport is stopped there); the 22 s note is the tool's simulated burst in both runs. vtitests 262/262. | E291 | 2026-10-20 | REAPER crash after a GUI render (E265/E277/E287) explained in REAPER's SWELL, caught 3 times under gdb with a watchpoint on SWELL's focused-window global (work/timing/e287-watch.py; REAPER 7.82 linux-x86_64, libSwell sha256 d40bc05a.., release 0.8.28 plug-in). Static (REA/Ghidra on libSwell, matched line by line against the open WDL source swell-generic-gdk.cpp / swell-dlg-generic.cpp, commit 9c8cab5): the crash function is SWELL_SetCursor (libSwell 0x158500), which calls gdk_window_set_cursor on the global SWELL_focused_oswindow (0x3c0418). SWELL clears that global when it destroys a window through swell_oswindow_destroy (0x150b80), but not on a second path: EndDialog keeps a closed modal dialog's OS window as a "spare" for the next dialog and detaches it from the dialog; a timer (100 ms, 500 ms while the app is inactive) then destroys it in swell_dlg_destroyspare (0x144430) with a bare gdk_window_destroy. Runtime (work/timing/e287-20261020-141122.log, -142238.log, X focus log e287-focus-142319.log): every render's progress window ("Rendering to file...", reused from the "Render Warning" window = the spare path) is destroyed by 0x144430; in the good cases the focus global had moved on before (REAPER refocused the Render to File dialog, or the window manager gave a REAPER window the X focus); in three cases (14:16:10.412, 14:25:27.857, 14:26:38.312) the spare was destroyed while it was still the focused window and nothing reset the global; twice the next cursor update (mouse movement, 2-5.5 s later) crashed with the E265 stack (same addresses), once a REAPER "Finished" window took the focus 27 ms later and rescued it. | E304 | 2026-10-21 | How the original hands a parameter change to the host without a mouse touch (issue #10: knobs turned on the TI2 are not added in Ableton Live's Configure mode). Static analysis (REA/Ghidra, read-only, sub-agent): the Windows VST3 "Virus TI.vst3" (x64) is a VST2-in-VST3 shim - it loads Common\Virus Control.dll (export VSTPluginMainPocketRocket, 0x180001020) and its audioMaster callback (0x180002850) maps opcode 0 (audioMasterAutomate) to IComponentHandler::performEdit, 43/44 (begin/end edit) to beginEdit/endEdit (handler at wrapper +0x30), 13/42 to restartComponent - no outputParameterChanges. Virus Control.dll is a VST2 AudioEffectX: setParameterAutomated 0x180001690 (setParameter + opcode 0), beginEdit 0x180002eb0 (43), endEdit (44). Class CVSTAutomation (vtable 0x18016f9c0, one slot per automatable parameter, the parameter instance points to its slot at +0x4A0): immediate setters (0x1800666d0, 0x180066630, 0x1800ad240 flag 0) send performEdit only; the deferred setter (0x1800667f0 -> 0x1800ad240 flag 1) queues the slot, and a periodic tick (0x1800ad4a0, GetTickCount, under a critical section) sends beginEdit + performEdit with the current value (one per tick, coalesced) and endEdit once 250 ms passed without a new change. GUI mouse gestures call beginEdit/endEdit directly (0x180027770 / 0x180027800). Patch/bank loads through the CRemoteDevice listener (0x180081980) take a setter that does not reach the automation code (MED: not every call site read). Not determined: which setter a single knob report from the Virus calls (deferred is the plausible one: it exists for changes without a touch, and Live registers the knob) | static analysis of the user's Virus TI.vst3 and Virus Control.dll | - | HIGH (wrapper mapping, AudioEffectX calls, CVSTAutomation tick and 250 ms), LOW-MED (which setter a knob report uses) |
| E305 | 2026-10-21 | macOS: installing a TI Revive package over an installed one of the same version left the plug-in bundles unchanged. MacBook Air M3: the CI package of run 39577249656 (0.8.29, built 2026-10-21 11:34) installed with `sudo installer -pkg` over the 0.8.29 test build of run 39477408077 (installed 2026-10-20): pkgutil receipt 0.8.29, install time 11:48, broker = the package's (sha256 e234fccc..), but VST3 (installed f8c05a04.., package 44e2d989..) and AU (e8a7e696.. vs a7d8a2fe..) still the old ones, file dates 2026-10-20 20:58. Cause: scripts/mac-build-release.sh builds the component plist with `pkgbuild --analyze`, whose default BundleIsVersionChecked = true makes the installer keep an installed bundle whose version is not older (both 0.8.29); only BundleIsRelocatable was switched off. Fix: BundleIsVersionChecked false for every bundle (the package always writes them). Affects test builds with an unchanged version and users who reinstall the same version (e.g. to repair); a newer version replaced the bundles as before (0.8.28 release install matched the package by sha256) | Mac receipt + checksums, package payload unpacked locally Verified 2026-10-21 12:15: the package of CI run 39582553971 (with the fix) installed over the 0.8.29 of run 39577249656 without removing anything replaced VST3 (b3706d1a..) and AU (a3526065..) = the package payload, dates of the new build | scripts/mac-build-release.sh | HIGH |

Root list (Win 0x18008c9a0, Mac 0xd63ce), in order: Personal = file://{mydocuments}/Access Music/Virus TI/Patches, User = {userpatches}/..., Public = {patches}/...; all three registered every time (0x18008d070 -> 0x18009f9e0, a name already registered is skipped). Placeholders: Windows 0x180092cc0 (SHGetFolderPath) - {mydocuments} = CSIDL_PERSONAL (Documents), {userpatches}/{user_appdata} = CSIDL_APPDATA (%APPDATA%), {patches}/{common_appdata} = CSIDL_COMMON_APPDATA (C:\ProgramData); macOS 0xd7db0 (FSFindFolder) - {mydocuments} = ~/Documents, {userpatches} = ~/Library/Application Support, {patches} = /Library/Application Support. So the Mac's factory libraries (installed by Access to /Library/Application Support/Access Music/Virus TI/Patches, the Tutorial's Mac path) are "Public Libraries"; on Windows the installer puts them in Documents ("Personal"). The bank menu (E217, 0x180041a10) adds the separator + "Create Empty Library" to Personal, or to Public when Personal is missing; "Create Empty Library" (Win 0x180041ac0, Mac 0x73f4c) always opens the save dialog in <Documents>/Access Music/Virus TI/Patches. (library_dir), so on the Mac it misses all factory libraries (round 0.8.29 M3: "The libraries not showing"; MacTest.mid landed in ~/Downloads - the save dialog did not start in ~/Documents, open: Live's Documents permission). (4) 'Audio clock changed': the Access driver follows the DAW rate - at a change it re-configures the device (SET_INTERFACE 0, SET_CONFIGURATION, SET_INTERFACE) and sends UAC SET_CUR sampling frequency (22 01 00 01 82 00 03 00 / ... 01 00 ... , 80 BB 00 = 48000 or 44 AC 00 = 44100) to EP 0x82 and 0x01, twice each; the iso stream then really runs at 48 kHz (958464 instead of 880588 bytes per 5 s = 48000/44100); the USB chip raises the message itself on an actual rate change (tusb 0x1868 -> 0x2dc). | E322 | 2026-10-22 | Why the original asks for RAM banks 2-4 twice at start-up (capture orig-startup-lcd-20261022, reply END times; static REA/Ghidra on Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64, nothing run). The bank check is a tick state machine: tick 0x18007ec30 -> repository step 0x18008ddd0 (drains the incoming queue, then runs 0x18008e220 when the time stamp CRepository+0x540 is reached) -> bank-info phase: the first bank with 'needs bank info' (CVirusBank+0x129, set for the RAM banks at mount 0x18008ded0) gets 0x18008ea30 = 74 01 7E 07 + 39 bb, next time now + 5 s; asking the same bank again counts as a retry (warning after 3, abort 0x18008e0d0 after 5). The reply entry 0x18008eaf0 accepts any 10/14 reply (267/524 B, byte 7 not 0), forwards it (bank handler 0x18009c150 clears +0x129, hands every program its fingerprint) and sets the time stamp to 0, so the next tick asks at once. One surplus reply therefore makes it ask for the next bank again while that bank's reply (267 B, ~0.5 s on USB) still arrives; the Virus answers both byte-identically and the chain runs to the last bank (each request ~25 ms after a reply's end). The second reply changes nothing (no comparison, same fingerprints); the browser panes are not in the send path. A side effect, not a feature: TI Revive keeps one request per bank (MemorySync::transact, matched by bank, inbox cleared); scripts/startup_diff.py counts the repeats as known. | E329 | 2026-10-23 | What the original does with notes between an audio restart (rate change) and the new sync marker (static, REA/Ghidra 12.1.4 on the user's Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64, sha256 a622f9a2..; for E328). (1) Send path 0x1800a86b0: every MIDI event is sent at once through 0x1800a80f0; a stamp (0xF5 0x53 + 14-bit frame) goes before it only when the timing option flag (object +0xa28, set only by the vtable setter 0x1800aa120, which logs one of two short strings) is on and a device exists - nothing is held or dropped while the timeline is unknown. (2) Stamp 0x1800a8420: stamp = device position (vtable +0x60 of the audio device) - reference (+0xa40) + sample offset x conversion ratio, plus a lead when no marker search runs; the "last stamp" (+0xf74) = -100000 skips the lateness check once. (3) Resume 0x1800a9880 (the restart after a rate change) sets +0xf74 = -100000 but does NOT touch the reference +0xa40. (4) The marker search 0x1800a8900 (input samples beyond +-0x300000) sets the reference +0xa40 = device position + index and ends the search; while it searches, the send path stretches its timeout to 44100 frames on every note-on and forces a sample offset >= 1. Consequence: in the window between the restart and the new marker the original stamps notes against the OLD reference and the restarted device position - its notes there are off as well (how far depends on the driver's position after the restart: not visible statically). TI Revive's two off notes after an in-run switch (E328) are the same kind of transition; holding notes until the new anchor would be our own improvement, not the original's behaviour | REA static analysis | work/rea/virus-control-5.1.7-x64.ghidra12.snapshot (local) | HIGH (send path never holds/drops, reference only set by the marker search, resume leaves it), MED (that the original's notes in the window are audibly off) |

| E331 | 2026-10-23 | Why a Virus can stay at 48 kHz although SET_CUR 44100 is accepted (E175/E241) - static, the USB chip (TUSB3200A 8051, tusbcode.bin) and host CPU bank 0, full instruction dumps from the existing Ghidra projects (read-only; nothing sent). (1) The chip's SET_CUR handler 0x1868 (EP 0x82 or 0x01) compares the requested rate (XDATA 0xfeb1/0xfeb0) with its stored rate INTMEM 0x7e/0x7f; when EQUAL it only acknowledges (bit 0x29) and marks "the PC owns the rate" (bit 0x10): no change flag 0x07, no countdown, nothing to the host CPU (HIGH). GET_CUR (0x0ee8, bmRequestType 0xA2, selector 1 -> 0x1a19) returns a copy of 0x7e/0x7f, the stored value, not a measurement (HIGH). Every chip-side writer of 0x7e/0x7f also sets the change flag (SET_CUR 0x1893, key 0x10 0x0650/0x066b, 0x1b43, link-up 0x03a9, auto-detect 0x022b, init 0x1b82/0x00ee); the main loop 0x02dc then sends 73 10 vv to the host CPU, plus 74 7E 00 ('Audio clock changed') only for a SET_CUR change (bit 0x0c) (HIGH). (2) Divergence path (MED mechanism, LOW that it is the E175 trigger): host CPU FUN_5953 sends its stored global 0x10 (XDATA 0x0410) straight to the DSPs (73 F4 F4 + value 10 40) WITHOUT telling the chip; callers: the reset routine FUN_4ee8 (0x5244) and the deferred job FUN_2bf9 (main loop FUN_1ca8, bit 0x4c set and XDATA 0x4f01 = 0; who sets 0x4c not followed). With 0x0410 = 1 the DSP PLL goes to 48 k while the chip's 0x7e/0x7f still says 44.1 - from then on every SET_CUR 44100 is a no-op and only a power cycle helps: the E175/E241 symptom. (3) Consequence for TI Revive's recovery (ratelever.h, since 0.8.27): SET_CUR 44100 x3 and the global 73 00 10 00 both meet an equal stored value and change nothing; a real switch 44.1 -> 48 -> 44.1 (ratesw.h, on hardware E328) makes the chip announce 73 10 01 / 73 10 00, and chip frames on cable 4 go to the host parser AND the DSPs (FUN_28e7 -> 0x277c / 0x605b, HIGH) - a candidate recovery, untested. (4) 0x1b43 (SA Freq) runs with the USB link UP (byte 0x0c = 0 at link-up 0x039e, 2 at link-down 0x03ca) - corrects E323 (6); it sets 0x7e/0x7f with the change flag, so a later SET_CUR 44100 would work: alone it does not explain E175. Corrections to E257: 0x10b0 is a compare routine against 0x7e/0x7f, not a variable; key 0xFF -> 0x06cd (0 clears bit 0x0e and sets 0x14, else sets 0x0e); unknown keys return. (5) `73 40 10 vv` from the Virus is most likely the DSP echoing FUN_5953's word (part byte 0x40, MED), `73 00 10 vv` the normal parameter path; how 0x3A/0x3B/0x3C route them not found. | E337 | 2026-10-24 | Crackle in stretches at a large sound-card buffer = the timeline's "jump back to real time" ping-ponging on host jitter (Windows bare metal, Ableton, Creative card at 44.1 kHz / 2048 without ASIO, Virus TI2 Desktop, round-0.8.32 CI test build; follow-up of E187). The plug-in's diag.log 07:44-08:04: Ableton passes 256-frame blocks (`host 44100 Hz block 256 (largest 256)`, as in E187); `drops 0` throughout, process time max 50-260 us and `over block time 0` in real time, `read margin min` never below 0 - so no USB loss, no CPU overrun, no underread. Instead four episodes of about a minute, roughly every 6 minutes (07:44:46-07:45:36, 07:51:31-07:52:21, 07:57:11-07:58:11, 08:03:01-08:04:01): per 10 s 195-587 timeline steps and 2-198 jumps (`anchors`), err -767..852; between them calm (steps 0-3, anchors 0, err -184..256). Two of them with the Virus sounding (raw peaks ~5000-6000); the user confirms the crackle came in stretches. Mechanism (code reading, native/plugin/source/virusplugin.cpp process(), timeline block ~1250-1310): err = (newest ring frame - S) - tl_ is taken from the instantaneous ring position per block; with a marker and abs(err) > 512 the read position jumps by err at once (tl_ += err, the branch made for REAPER's real offsets, E279), else one-frame steps when abs(err) > 256. When the host's block cadence turns uneven, err swings about +-800 frames within a second, so it jumps one way, then back - up to ~20 jumps/s, each skipping or repeating 12-19 ms of audio (a click); the 1-frame steps (up to ~59/s) add small discontinuities. What makes Ableton's cadence uneven every ~6 min is not known (background task, the Creative driver or Ableton). The same day's Windows hil-win.ps1 run (H1, H2-1, H2-2, H3, H6) passed, err within -445..156, no jumps Fix (round-0.8.33, 2026-10-24, native/src/device/timelinefollow.h, used by process()): a jump needs the error beyond 512 on one side for 0.5 s (jump by its mean over that time), or a 2 s mean beyond 512 (an offset hidden in a rough cadence), or abs(err) > 4096 (at once: REAPER processing ahead); one-frame drift steps start when the 2 s mean is beyond 256 and stop at 128. The anchor and the 8192 re-anchor are unchanged. Python model of the same arithmetic (ring in 441-frame chunks, 256-frame blocks; bursts of 8 blocks with +-20 ms jitter and a 40 ms late burst every ~5 s, 60 s): old rule ~3700 jumps and ~3000 steps, new rule 0 jumps and 149-199 steps (8 seeds); a lasting 1000-frame offset: old jumps at once, new one jump after 0.50 s; the same offset in a rough cadence: old ~1930 jumps, new one jump after ~2 s; drift +-200 ppm: no jumps, err -496..485. The model became native/tests/test_timelinefollow.cpp. Linux 2026-10-24 (Virus TI2 Desktop, dev build of round-0.8.33): built (a name clash with the existing follow_ fixed: the member is tl_follow_), 389 unit tests pass; with the old per-block rule put back in the header 3 of the 6 timeline tests fail (rough cadence, jump timing, offset in a rough cadence). Reproduced on the device with nativehost --rough 15:75 (new: bursts of 8 x 256 frames, +-20 ms jitter, one burst 40 ms late every ~5 s; harsher than Ableton: err -2100..2780), 80 s with notes each second: old rule 315-627 jumps and 265-460 steps per 10 s, notes skipping +-480 ms; new rule 0 jumps, 0-249 steps per 10 s, no skips (notes in the rough window 5-22 ms late, never early: the cadence wakes bursts at their middle, up to ~60 ms behind, a note cannot be stamped in the past; a real host at 2048 runs ahead). REAPER-like offsets (nativehost --ahead 100@20 --ahead 25@40, even cadence): new 1 + 1 jumps, only the note at each moment off (+97 / +14 ms), the next on the grid; old 6 + 2 jumps, the same notes +103 / +29 ms. scripts/hil.sh on the dev build: ALL PASS (H1, H2 x2, H3 1.00x, H6). Windows PC 2026-10-24 afternoon (CI test build 0.8.33, run 40093015139; Ableton, Creative card 44.1 kHz, host blocks 256): hil-win.ps1 results-20261024-142018 ALL PASS (H1, H2 x2, H3, H6; graded on Linux). Ableton session 14:25:16-14:32:07 (7 min, one instance, user: crackle gone; at the end 3 presets with dense MIDI, some notes seemed missing): 1 jump (the start anchor), then 0; drops 0, USB errors 0, process time max ~270 us, MIDI queue 0; err -367..73 per 10 s until 14:28:49, then a milder uneven stretch to 14:30:29 (err max up to 509) answered by 134 one-frame steps once (old rule: steps per block, no jumps below 512), then -65..375. (1) The note stamps do NOT depend on a part's output: stamp 0x1800a8420 uses the channel only for per-channel offset bookkeeping (+0xa74/+0xe74); the lead is one global value (+0xa6c, flag +0xa6b; setters 0x1800aa1a0/0x1800aa180, fed from the registry values UseLuxuryLatency / LuxuryLatencyOffset by 0x18006ddf0). (2) 0x180084970 walks the 16 parts: Output Select below 9 (Out1/2/3) = analog; stores per-part analog flags + dirty flags in the device object (+0x1e1119.., +0x1e1129..) and a delay in ms (+0x1e113c; a change marks all parts dirty); 0x18002f9f0 (a vtable method) converts a sample count to ms (samples x 1000 / rate) and calls it; 0x1800a9c20, at the start of every audio block (from process 0x1800aa7c0), sends for each dirty part `F0 00 20 33 01 10 6F pp 7F ms F7` (ms = the delay for analog parts, 0 for USB parts); 0x1800a7770 resets flags and delay at connect. Inference (MEDIUM): the sample count is the plug-in's reported latency (0x180029af0 sets LuxuryLatencyOffset 0xC00 = 3072, copies it to Latency, calls setInitialDelay); the vtable slot of 0x18002f9f0 is not identified. (3) Registry also has AnalogOutputDelay (default 0) with no reader found (maybe unused); 0x18006ed70 sets "Multi Parameters/Output Delay" to 0 for all parts (link to 6F..7F a guess, LOW). Also 0x18002ea50 remaps USB3 parts to USB1 when the third USB output is off. Wire: captures/ref-mastervol-3 (all parts on USB) has the original sending `6F pp 7F 00` 80 times (all 16 parts, value 0, at Play); TI Revive never sends it (captures/trv-analog-1: 0) - so with TI Revive analog parts play undelayed, i.e. early against the USB parts (the user heard analog and USB parts out of sync, 2026-10-24). FIX 2026-10-24: default_page_b() picks the position by binary format (PE: init - 0x400; thin 64-bit or universal Mach-O: init + 0x2a0); tests accessfiles_bundle_dumps (Mach-O layout with a 7-bit decoy at init - 0x400) and accessfiles_pe_page_b; on the real files (vtimodel pageb) the Mac bundle now gives the same 256 bytes as the Windows x64 DLL. Cause (code): DeviceOut::run() took the ring's rate (rate = audio_.rate()) only on the first audio start after the broker connection; the retry every 500 ms (used when that start failed, here: Virus still off) opened the ring without it, so DeviceOut::rate stayed at its default 44100 while the ring ran at 48000 - the timeline counted 9 % too few frames per block and re-anchored about twice a second; setActive's rate request then found the broker already at 48000 (no rate_gen step, so rate_changed() never corrected it). Since rate following step 5 (ed4f5b8, 2026-10-23; v0.8.33 has it). Not caused by the timeline review fixes of the same morning (E351, reverted). Fix: DeviceOut::start_audio(), one function for the first start and the retries, which always takes the rate. | E354 | 2026-10-25 | How the original fills the LCD on hover (static, REA/Ghidra 12.1.4 on the user's Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64, sha256 a622f9a2..; described in our own words). The editor frame (KFrame, constructor 0x18003c180) looks up five UI parameters once: VirusControl/Display Parameter, Display Value, Display Original Value, Display Part, Display MIDI Message (frame fields 0x148..0x168). The hover handler 0x18003cbd0 gets the control under the mouse: if it is a parameter control (KControlEx with a parameter object), it takes the control's part (or the selected part when the control has none) and the parameter's name; parameters under "VirusControl..." and under "Internal..." are skipped, except "Internal MIDI Standard Parameters". Then it sets Display Parameter = the name, Display Value = the parameter's current text for that part, Display Original Value = "[" + the text of a second per-part value of the parameter + "]" (parameter class vtable +0xb8 -> the per-part instance's value field, 0x1800679d0 -> 0x180066b00, written by the clamping setters 0x180066a40/0x180066aa0), Display Part = "Part " + number; each field is written only when its text changed, and a flag (frame +0x170) remembers that hover filled them. Off a parameter control with that flag set, all four fields are set to an empty string and the flag cleared. Not resolved statically: when that per-part value is taken anew (the user saw it stay at the patch's value after edits, E353) | REA evidence ev_1a08dfeb.. (constructor), ev_82fdeffa.. Both only look at items of the drag container's text type (2): drag-move offers a drop only for text items and only when the table's bank can be written; the drop handler reads the text items (the browser's own patch entries, dragged within the plug-in) and returns without doing anything for any other type. Static, REA/Ghidra 12.1.4 on the user's Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64 (sha256 a622f9a2..): CPatchLibrarySMFDecoder (vtable 0x180167dd8), decode 0x18006b360, walks the SysEx events of one track and keeps an event only if its length is exactly 266 or 523 bytes after the F0 (a 267- or 524-byte dump), it starts 00 20 33 01 and byte 5 is 0x10 (single dump; the device byte is not checked). It reads the name from the dump (10 characters) and the version byte, but does not check the version. Every other length is skipped without a message (a header mismatch goes to a debug/assert path). The receive path from the Virus (0x18007aa50) has the same 524/267 rule. The user's libraries (Linux, read-only Python SMF parse): "Yoz & Andy/Incubation V3 by Manuel Schleis.mid.mid" holds 54 SysEx events of 158 bytes and 43 of 202 (SMF event lengths, ending in F7, about 64-108 bytes cut out of the middle) next to 31 complete ones = a damaged file; Cosmosis Virus B 2 x 158, 2 x 70, 1 x 114; Nice Bytes Toxic one of 263. TI Revive keeps the same dumps as the original (31 of 128 for Incubation V3). One difference on purpose: a 267-byte dump with a version byte TI Revive's upgrade does not know (127 in "Howard Scarr Sounds", 1 patch) stays out; the original would list it, but what it sends to the Virus then is unknown (rule 2). Design: a .mid/.midi/.syx file dragged from the desktop onto either browser pane is imported into Personal Libraries (library_dir(), e.g. Documents/Access Music/Virus TI/Patches): a .mid/.midi byte for byte (as .mid), a .syx of up to 128 patches written as the original's .mid (save_library; the original reads no .syx), a larger .syx as it is; never over an existing file (free name "X 2", "X 3" ...); the same patches already there (bank/program bytes and checksums ignored), or a file from inside that folder, are only shown; a file without Virus patches is not copied. The library is listed at once, the pane it was dropped on switches to it, the LCD says what happened ("Library added" or "Added (N damaged)" (E356) / "Library already there" with the name below, "Library not added" + a short reason); while a library file is over a browser the LCD shows "Drop to add a library" / "(Personal Libraries)" (the LCD's second line holds 20 characters - longer first texts were cut off in the user's REAPER test, so the texts were shortened; unit test: import errors <= 20) and the cursor the copy sign, other files or other places get no drop. Dropping on a RAM slot does not write the Virus (rule 2: up to 128 dumps by accident); the existing drag from the library onto RAM does that. Code: import_library / importable_library (native/src/access/library.cpp, unit test library_import), VirusPlugin::import_library_file + lcd_message, EditorCanvasView::getDropTarget (VSTGUI 4 IDropTarget: OLE on Windows, NSView on macOS, XDND on X11; VSTGUI's X11 frame sets XdndAware). | E358 | 2026-10-25 | Desktop file drops in REAPER on Linux need two fixes in VSTGUI's X11 drop code (vstgui4 x11dragging.cpp, also unchanged in Steinberg's develop branch on this date). (1) REAPER's window library SWELL (WDL swell-generic-gdk.cpp, open source) receives the drop on its own XdndAware container window and forwards XdndEnter/Position/Drop/Leave to the embedded plug-in window with itself as source, but announces XDND protocol version 3; VSTGUI's enter() returns for versions below 5, so the whole drag was ignored. Trace (DndTrace switched on locally): our window 0x4e00001 (parent = REAPER's container 0x4c00078, both XdndAware 5) got Position, Drop, Leave but no logged Enter (the log line comes after the version check). Accepting version >= 3 made the drop work (the fields VSTGUI reads exist since version 3; 4 added XdndProxy, 5 two fields in XdndFinished). (2) VSTGUI stores the XdndPosition only while the data is requested, so every later move and the drop used the position the drag came in at: the user's drops always went to the lower pane. Storing each position fixed it. Both as native/patches/vstgui-x11-xdnd.patch, applied by native/CMakeLists.txt at configure time on Linux only (git apply, skipped when it reverses cleanly; Windows/Mac code untouched). User test 2026-10-25 (REAPER, GNOME Wayland/XWayland, file manager drag): drop works, the pane under the mouse is used, other places get no drop: 'works!' | trace log + window tree (xwininfo/xprop) + SWELL source + user test | native/patches/vstgui-x11-xdnd.patch, native/CMakeLists.txt | HIGH (REAPER/Linux; other Linux hosts not tested) |
| E359 | 2026-10-25 | Issue #16 (E336) re-read: the reporter's macOS report (FL Studio 26.1.6, 0.8.31 AU, crash 21:20:22 +0300) has a thread 31 waiting in __wait4 whose caller frame (0x10979b404) lies in NO loaded image - TI Revive's own code, already unloaded: on macOS the plug-in starts the broker itself (broker.cpp start_broker) and a detached thread waits in waitpid for it; when the broker exits (30 s after its last client) that thread returns into the unloaded binary. This proves code of ours outlives the unload; it is NOT shown to be the fault of #16 (that thread was still waiting; FL reports a bus error on a side thread, the macOS report only shows FL's later abort; the open window as trigger points rather at the editor's Cocoa view released after the unload). Fix for both (round 0.8.35): on macOS the plug-in binary takes one extra reference on itself at load (entry.cpp KeepLoaded: dladdr + dlopen RTLD_NOLOAD\|RTLD_NODELETE), so the AU wrapper's CFBundleUnloadExecutable no longer unmaps it; hosts that keep plug-ins loaded behave as before. Technique checked on Linux (glibc: a test library stays mapped after dlclose with it, is unmapped without it). REPRODUCED on the Mac (2026-10-25, MacBook Air M3, macOS 26.6.2, installed release 0.8.34 AU, autest --delete-open: view open, view removed but not yet released, unit disposed - FL's order): SIGSEGV in objc_msgSend at address 0 from objc_autoreleasePoolPop, 4/4 runs; the crashing object (x0) is the editor's VSTGUI_NSView, still 9 times in the autorelease pool (_objc_autoreleasePoolPrint); dyld still lists plugin.vst3 (images with Objective-C code stay mapped), so the cause is not unmapped code but the wrapper's dlclose running the plug-in's C++ static destructors - among them VSTGUI's RuntimeObjCClass<VSTGUI_NSView>, which calls objc_disposeClassPair while an instance of that class waits in the pool (inference from the VSTGUI source + the class-less crash; no zombie message, as expected for a disposed class). The waitpid thread (above) is a second, later path. With the inner plug-in pinned from outside (AUTEST_PIN=1: dlopen RTLD_NOLOAD\|RTLD_NODELETE on plugin.vst3 before the dispose = what entry.cpp does itself) the same 0.8.34 binary runs clean 3/3, including 40 s after the dispose. | E364 | 2026-10-26 | How the original keeps notes in time in every host (REA, Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64, Ghidra 12.1.4; for E363 'late notes in Logic'). CORRECTED by E400: the lead and the reported latency are 3072 + base (3840 + block), not 3072; notes played live skip the lead. (1) Stamp (0x1800a8420): stamp = the audio device's CURRENT position (vtable +0x60 of the device object, read when the host calls process) - the marker anchor (+0xa40) + the event's sample offset x (device rate / host rate); with UseLuxuryLatency (+0xa6b) the fixed lead (+0xa6c) is added and `F5 53 lo hi` goes before the event, one per distinct stamp; a stamp earlier than the previous one is reported and starts a new sync-marker search (0x1800a8880). So every note lies the full lead ahead of 'now' whenever the host calls - a late or bursty block cannot make it late; the host's block timing only shifts the note by the call-time jitter. (2) The lead: 0x180029af0 registers Latency and LuxuryLatencyOffset (default 4000) but at every start forces LuxuryLatencyOffset = 0xC00 = 3072 frames, copies it to Latency and reports it with setInitialDelay(3072): lead = reported latency = 3072 frames on every host and block size (0x18006ddf0 passes UseTimeStamps / UseLuxuryLatency / LuxuryLatencyOffset / UseSampleRateConverter to the device object at connect). (3) A host table (0x1801c5f20, 0x90-byte entries: vendor, product, minimum version, a value, flags HostDoesTimeInfoLatencyCompensation / HostDoesEventLatencyCompensation / 'MIDI events are real time' / one more; applied in 0x18002d540, inverted for versions below the minimum): FXpansion wrappers, Emagic Logic (0/0), Cubase >= 4500, Cubase VST >= 7000, Nuendo >= 3000, Live >= 5000 (real-time flag), Apple Logic >= 959143 (1/1), GarageBand, Digital Performer >= 6000 / >= 4500 / older (0/0), Fruity Wrapper >= 6400 / older (0/0), Cakewalk VST >= 4. Used in the transport/clock code (0x1800303b0: getTimeInfo, song position F2, clock, FA/FB/FC): a host without time-info compensation gets the song position shifted by the latency (3072 frames as beats), the event offset +-latency when the two flags differ - musical sync (arpeggiator, clock), not note lateness. TI Revive differs: stamps from the host timeline (ring frame t + S + A, plugin.h lead()), A = 1024 on Linux/macOS (3072 on Windows since the VM had late notes) - a host that calls late or in bursts (Logic, E363) can make a stamp lie in the past -> late notes and the sync error | REA static analysis | work/rea/virus-control-5.1.7-x64.ghidra12.snapshot (local) | HIGH (stamp rule, lead 3072 forced, host table contents), MED (device position = the driver's real-time position: from the call site and E329, the device class not decompiled) |

The original is a VST2 and gets every MIDI event; its state keeps CC 1..16 per channel (docs/formats.md). (2) The part's name field stayed white after a double click + Enter without a change and hid every later name (screenshot in #18). Cause: VSTGUI's CTextEdit reports a commit (valueChanged) only for a changed text (ctextedit.cpp updateText), and the editor removed the field only there. Fix: every name field registers the text-edit listener and is removed when it loses the focus. Test: same setup, user: Enter, Esc and a click elsewhere remove the field, names update - 'works'. (3) Linux only: the selected text in a name field was red - VSTGUI's own text field (generictextedit.cpp, used on X11) marks a selection in the frame's focus colour, red by default; the editor sets it to light blue (179, 215, 255), focus frames stay off (user: 'works', REAPER, Linux). | E368 | 2026-10-26 | How the original handles MIDI from the host (towards #18: are controller values kept?). REA/Ghidra, Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64 (a622f9a2..): the VST2 dispatcher (0x180001c80) sends effProcessEvents (opcode 0x19) to CVSTPlugIn's method at vtable +0x268 = 0x18002ffa0. It takes MIDI and SysEx events of channels below the device's part count; channel aftertouch is merged per channel (only a changed value goes on); everything else, controllers included, goes to CRemoteDevice 0x18007aa50 with origin 2. That function sends the message to the Virus (through 0x1800815e0 and the device queue) AND, for a status byte A0..F0, pushes it into the device's CIntermediateMIDIQueue (0x180031c20; ring of 25000 entries at device +0xccdc8, set up in the constructor 0x18006ce00). 0x180080740 drains that ring and runs every message through every MIDI message definition of the device description (0x180062070: length, then each content element) - the decoder that turns messages into parameter values. So a host controller changes the model's value of every parameter the description maps to it: the Internal MIDI Standard Parameters (mod wheel, volume, ...) and the page A synth parameters alike; the state (docs/formats.md: CC 1..16 per channel and the part dumps) then holds them. Not followed to the leaf: the content element's apply method (vtable +0x10). TI Revive (0.8.35, 8690cf8 narrowed to the Standard Parameters, now every decoded parameter): a mapped controller is decoded with the device description and set like a Virus report (set_part_value). Practical check (win10ref, Ableton Live 11, the original, user 2026-10-26): a MIDI clip with a CC 1 clip envelope 0 -> 127, played, saved; the state decoded from the .als: (a) the original VST3 (Virus TI.vst3): all 224 controller records at their defaults (CC 1 = 0) - its VST3 maps only aftertouch and pitch bend (formats.md, 32 MIDI-mapping targets), so Live never hands it CC 1: #18 also happens with the original VST3 in Live; (b) the original VST2 (VSTPlugins/Access Music/Virus TI.dll): ch 1 CC 1 = 107 (the last value before the stop), all others default -> the original keeps a host controller's value and saves it. TI Revive (99cd092, Linux, REAPER, TI2, user 2026-10-26): the same in the state decoded from the .RPP when saved during playback - ch 1 CC 1 = 80 and part 1's dump byte 10 (page A #1) = 80, the original VST2's pattern (CC 1 = 107 and dump byte 10 = 107). Saved after a stop it holds 0: REAPER sends CC 1 = 0 itself at the stop (virusmon, right before the note-off), Live does not. Not checked in practice: a page A synth parameter sent as a controller (same decoder, static only) | REA static analysis (addresses above), the user's Live sets cc1.als / cc1-vst2.als decoded (not in git) | native/plugin/source/virusplugin.cpp (drain_param_updates) | HIGH (the Standard Parameters: static + practical), MEDIUM (page A parameters: static only) |

| E371 | 2026-10-26 | How the original's graphic editor controls react to the mouse (towards #19: filter/envelope drags, ARP user pattern, ARP actions). Static, REA/Ghidra subagent on Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64 (a622f9a2..), nothing run. Chain: 0x1801346c0 skin type ids (ADSTREnvelope 0x18, ArpEdit 0x19, LP4/HP4/BP4/BS4Response 0x1d..0x20), 0x180058a10 creates by id, 0x18005b1e0 binds parameters. (1) Filter response displays are KEnvelope instances (LP4 0x180038240, HP4 0x1800383f0, BP4 0x1800385a0, BS4 0x180038750): every stage X = the bound parameter (Cutoff or Offset for FilterLink), Y = Filter N/Resonance; drag right = higher cutoff, up = more resonance; BS4 has no Y. Mouse: down 0x18003b270, up 0x18003b4f0, moved 0x18003b580, hit test 0x18003ae00: relative (start value + pixel delta / (px per unit x stage scale; X 127, Y 57 units), so the handle follows the mouse 1:1, no jump), Shift = 4x finer (re-anchors when a modifier changes), clamped; Ctrl+click resets the grabbed parameters to the stage defaults (cutoff 0.5, resonance 0); no double-click handling; hit: the handles first (handle bitmap size centred, else 4x4), then each segment's bounding box (at least +-2 px), a segment hit goes to a following 'linked' stage; begin/end edit around the drag. (2) ADSTREnvelope (0x180038070, 6 stages): stage 1 X = Attack, 2 X = Decay, 3 Y = Sustain, 4 Y = Sustain Time, 5 X = Release; the decay end point also moves Sustain vertically (linked Y), the sustain start point Sustain, the slope end Sustain Time (up = rising), the release end Release; scales 127 (Sustain Time 63); same mouse code (relative, Shift finer, Ctrl+click = 0.5 defaults, MEDIUM). (3) ArpEdit = KStepSequencer (down 0x18004e500, moved 0x18004e7d0, up 0x18004e6d0, hit 0x18004e100, layout 0x18004dee0): cell = width/32; an active step's area = its cell plus the following steps that are off; bar from cell left + 2 over (span - 2) x length, height = velocity; a click/drag only on an active step's area: length when x is within 8 px left of the bar end or right of it, velocity when y is above 8 px below the bar top, both in the corner; relative (length += dx/span, velocity -= dy/height), clamped, stays on the grabbed step (no painting); Alt+click on any cell toggles that step's Bitfield (the only way to switch a step); hover highlights and sets the cursor; no Ctrl reset. (4) ARP actions (registered 0x18006ed70; posted from 0x180081980 when the parameter becomes 1; context menu 0x1800684d0 on every 'Arpeggiator User Pattern/...' control; executed in 0x180084c90; L = Arpeggiator/UserPatternLength + 1; rand()/32768): RandomNote/'Randomize Pattern' - step 1 on, steps 2..L random on/off, every 4th off since the last forced on forced on; RandomVelo - velocity 1..L uniform random; RandomLength - length 1..L uniform random; ShiftRight/Left - steps 1..L rotate by one (bitfield, velocity, length together); 'Reset Pattern' (menu) - steps 1..L to defaults; 'Fill Up Pattern' (menu) - copies 1..L into L+1..32 repeatedly, then doubles the length if it fits. No patch-wide randomize (rand has one caller). (A) Segment hits (KEnvelope hit test 0x18003ae00, mouse down 0x18003b270, moved 0x18003b580; stage records 0xB0 bytes with axis flags, grab flags, X/Y link modes, start and end point; layout 0x180039480 copies each stage's start from the previous end): segment i runs from point i-1 to point i (stage 0 = origin, never hit); its box is the two end points, widened by 2 px on each side where it is under 4 px wide or tall; hit order: all handles first, then the segments, both from the last stage down (the later stage wins on overlap). A hit returns stage i; X and Y each walk forward over following stages with link mode 2; a parameter is grabbed only if the stage has that axis and its grab flag. Result per segment (tables 0x180153dd0 ADSTR, 0x180153ec0 LP, 0x180153ecc/f60 HP, 0x180153ff8 BP, 0x180154094 BS): ADSTR attack -> Attack, decay -> Decay + Sustain, sustain -> Sustain, slope -> Sustain Time, release -> Release; LP flat part -> Cutoff, rise to the peak -> Cutoff + Resonance, fall -> nothing; HP rise -> Cutoff + Resonance, the rest nothing; BP rise -> both, fall nothing; BS the two parts left of the notch bottom -> Cutoff, the right two nothing. Segment drags use the handle code (relative, Shift 4x finer with re-anchor, Ctrl+click = the grabbed stages' defaults). No cursor/hover handling found in KEnvelope. Built (TI Revive 0.8.35): EditorRenderer::graph_segments / graph_grabs / graph_grab (handles within 5 px first - our own tolerance - then the segment boxes, last first), graph_hit_test counts segments (a segment wins over the cutoff knob like a handle; one that drags nothing does not, so the knob keeps those clicks as in rc6 - the original's behaviour where the display overlaps the knob is not known), the editor drags a segment with the parameters of the handle at its end, anchored at that handle; test editor_graph_segment_grabs (433 tests). (B) Fade (KControlEx 0x180036ee0): opacity at +0x60, linear fade flags +0x74/+0x75 and tick counts +0x64/+0x68 (95/255 <-> 1.0), repaint-driven (0x180037160 keeps the view dirty while a flag is set); but no code was found that starts a fade - the only stores to the flags are the constructors (0x1800365e0, copy 0x180034700; search bounded to disp8 stores), and the first draw starts at the final opacity. So in 5.1.7 an active-state change most likely switches between 1.0 and 95/255 at once, which is what TI Revive does (E360); no fade built. Text/foreground fading not followed (moot if no fade runs) | REA static analysis (subagent report) | native/src/editor/editorrender.* (graph_segments, graph_grab), native/plugin/source/viruseditor.cpp | HIGH (A), MEDIUM (B: no fade starter found, bounded search) |
