Setup: Linux, REAPER 7.82/7.83, TI2, the user's project multi-syth-test (a MIDI track and three tracks sending MIDI to the plug-in on channels 1-3; its items start with CC10 64, CC7 100, RPN 0 = 12 on every channel). Debug logs (VTI_NATIVE_DEBUG: every send with stamp and data bytes, the arrival of each report) and an automated REAPER run (a Lua script passed on REAPER's command line: 10 x Play 4 s / Stop 2 s, then quit). (1) No message was stamped late: every send >= ~2100 frames ahead of the newest ring frame; the safety net held stamps instead of letting them go back. (2) REAPER calls setActive(0) and setActive(1) at every Play; the plug-in's setActive(0) sent CC64 0 + CC123 0 on all 16 channels (32 messages), stamped at the same stamp as the Play block that followed (F2, 73 00 23 01, REAPER's chased controllers, the first notes). Runs (reports after the start / 10 Plays): output delays only on change: 10, 10 (and 10, 10 after moving the restart set after the notes - the order makes no difference); the same without the 32 messages (temporary switch): 0, 0; with the original's output-delay set at every Play/Stop (E344): 6, 2, 4, 6. Fix (heldnotes.h): process() records held keys (note on/off) and sustain pedals (CC64 from the host mapping) per channel; setActive(0) releases only those (pedal up, then all notes off on that channel) - at REAPER's Play nothing is held (REAPER sends its note-offs at Stop), so nothing goes out. With the fix: only on change 0, 0; default (the original's set at Play/Stop) 3, 4, every one right after the set at Stop (the set delays what follows, E280; the clock runs on while stopped). (3) Controls tried on nativehost (TI2) without a report: setActive cycles while playing, with and without --ahead; loop restarts with F2; REAPER's controller block 20 ms before notes; loop restarts plus the block. (4) Part volume to 127 at Stop (user): REAPER sends CC6 0, CC7 127, CC10 64 on the channels whose controllers it chased from the items at Play (CC7 100 etc.); a copy of the project without those controller events: no CC7 at Stop (5 of 5 Stops vs 0). The plug-in passes host controllers on since 0.8.35 (#18, E368). The REAPER preference (2026-10-27, REAPER 7.83): 'Reset MIDI CC/Pitch to neutral on playback start and/or stop' (Preferences > Audio > Playback; its neutral for CC7 is 127) with the field 'CC reset overrides' (ini key ccresettab, REAPER's own help: 'cc7=127 ... -1 prevents reset'); the automated run with ccresettab=cc7=-1: no CC7 at Stop (0 of 10 Stops, 10 of 10 without) - README/site note for REAPER users. | E376 | 2026-10-27 | Voice count / 'notes dropped' (user question: show the voices playing and warn at the limit): the Virus keeps voice bookkeeping per DSP but never sends it - result (b): it exists, but is not reachable without new firmware or untested commands. Static only (subagent, TI2 DSP image + host CPU banks, listings in work/firmware, not in git; nothing sent to a device). (1) Per DSP 55 voice slots (0x37 x 0x32 words from X/Y:$0050; Y:+$2C state: 0xFC free, 0xFD retiring, 0xFE/0xFF releasing; X:+$2C owning part, X:+$2D age from X:$49235) - HIGH. Allocation 0x5340b scans for a free slot, +1 on the per-part count X:(part+$118) (0x5342b); with no free slot 0x53418 returns and the note is dropped without flag, counter or report - HIGH. Free 0x53658 (-1 at 0x5366c). Other counters: X:$5412e allocated voice records (+1 0x544b3, -1 0x5457d; called from the note-on path, so mostly per voice - revises E262's 'part set-up records', MEDIUM), X:$1f3e voices started, X:$54142 voices being force-released (a level, not an event count) - MEDIUM. The '20-90 voices' come from a load governor: 0x55267 measures how far rendering lags the audio ring (X:$55248, threshold 0x40); 0x548ea sums a load estimate and, at 0x40 or more, releases a victim (0x544ee -> 0x54546 = the steal); admission 0x546a7 holds back a new voice at 0x7F (branch 0x54796 not read) - MEDIUM. (2) No path out - HIGH for what was checked: none of the DSP's senders through the host-port queue 0x523b9 (SysEx sender 0x52484 incl. the 73 00 54 event and page 0x70/0x71 reports, beat flag 0x531bb, 0x53180, multi block dump 0x529cd, uncalled 0x5503f) reads these words; a pointer scan of all DSP records finds them only as instruction operands, never in a parameter/request table; the host CPU's DSP interrupt knows only F5, F4 and pass-through frames (E251) and answers dump requests from its own model; no voice or load string on the front panel ('/Voices' = the Unison parameter). (3) The only theoretical route, the host's private F4 path into the DSP (0x5256c, E248), is no read-back interface and is excluded by rule 2. So TI Revive cannot show the Virus' real voice count or warn about dropped notes; a count of the notes the plug-in itself holds would be an estimate of requested notes only. Not checked: the victim choice in detail, the admission branch, master/slave load exchange, the Snow image | static analysis of the user's firmware.bin (TI2 image), subagent report | - | HIGH (no path out; drop without report), MEDIUM (counter meanings, governor) |
| E377 | 2026-10-29 | macOS: the Virus streams at the other rate than Core Audio reports, and the plug-in believed Core Audio. Setup: MacBook Air M3, macOS 26.6, TI2, CI test build 0.8.35 (run 40745427793), Logic Pro (Snow AU) then Ableton Live 12 (two plug-in instances, one owner). Plug-in diag.log (stats every 10 s: 'host frames' = host blocks, 'virus frames' = frames Core Audio delivered into the ring): Logic 48 kHz: 480256 / 480256, clean. Logic quit 06:48:38, Live opened 06:48:51 (coreaudiod: the Virus' IO stopped, then started again; no overlap): host 48000 Hz, Core Audio nominal 48000 (system_profiler 'Current SampleRate: 48000'), but 441344 Virus frames per 10 s -> 20 re-anchors per 10 s, ~8 sync reports per second, the sync page (>3000 reports in 7 min). Live restarted at 44.1 kHz: 441216 host / 480256 Virus frames, the same. After a power cycle of the Virus, Live at 44.1: again 441088 / 480256, 20 anchors. A rate change in Live (to 48, back to 44.1: the plug-in's request_rate, 'asked on the Core Audio device') fixed it: 480000 / 479744 and 441216 / 441344, 0 anchors. So Core Audio's nominal rate can be stale against the Virus (cf. E331: the USB chip ignores a SET_CUR with its stored rate; E175/E241 the broker's relock), and AudioIn::open() set the rate only when the nominal one differed. Fix (TDD): ratemeter.h measures the ring's frames per second; two whole seconds at the other rate -> vti_relock_step (ratesw.h, the broker's recovery: away, one second later back, three tries, then a log line asking for a power cycle) on the Core Audio device (AudioIn::check_rate, worker loop). Not a 0.8.35 regression (audioin.cpp unchanged since the rate following, before 0.8.34). Which event leaves Core Audio and the Virus apart (Logic, the power cycle) not found. Fixed build (CI run 40827916741, pkg 722fc4af..): hil-mac ALL PASS (results-20261029-072801, max 0.88 ms, export 1.00x); after a power cycle Live at 44.1 started matched (441216 / 441344, 0 anchors, 2 sync reports in the session) - the mismatch did not come back, so the relock itself has not run on hardware | user Mac session, plug-in diag.log, coreaudiod log, Live Log.txt (scratchpad, not in git) | native/src/device/ratemeter.h, native/src/device/audioin.cpp (check_rate, switch_rate), native/plugin/source/virusplugin.cpp (DeviceOut::run), native/tests/test_ratemeter.cpp | HIGH (symptom, the manual fix), MEDIUM (cause: stale nominal rate), open (the relock on hardware: not reproduced on the fixed build) |

| E381 | 2026-11-01 | How the original Virus Control 5.1.7 fills the ROM banks, and user-flashed ROM banks (issue #22: own banks flashed into ROM with the original Control Center; TI Revive shows the factory names and, on a program chosen on the Virus, sends the factory dump - cause in TI Revive: ROM banks only from firmware.bin, native/src/access/firmware.h, never compared with the device). Static, REA/Ghidra sub-agent on Virus Control.dll x64 (sha256 a622f9a2...). (1) Bank list (0x18009c660): RAM A-D = bank 1..4, ROM A.. = bank 5.., 26 ROM banks when the flash block is valid. Each ROM bank has two firmware.bin chunks (0xC0+2i, 0xC1+2i; loader 0x1800af5d0). Open path 0x18009bbf0: a factory ROM bank is built from firmware.bin (bank byte 5+i, checksums redone); a bank marked "user" (or without firmware data) is loaded from its cache file, else its slots stay "not synced yet". (2) Which banks are "user": the model scan 0x1800ac7c0 takes the identification block at FF7FA0 (the same read-only reads as E026/E191, flashinfo.h); when 'FLSH' is at FF7FC0, a big-endian u32 at FF7FD2..FF7FD5 is turned into letters A..Z (bits 31..6, around 0x18007fb69) and passed in the device URL (`virus://<id>/<RAM n>/<ROM n>/<letters>[/sync]`, parsed in 0x18008d070); a ROM bank whose letter is listed gets the user flag and skips firmware.bin. Our factory TI2 Desktop (tirevive-cc info --raw, 2026-11-01): 'FLSH' + stamp at FF7FC0, FF7FD2..D5 = 00 00 00 00 (no letter, as expected for factory ROMs). (3) Bank info (39) is sent only for RAM banks (flag set in 0x18008ded0, phase in 0x18008e220); for every bank, RAM and ROM, slots not valid yet are fetched with 74 01 7E 04 + single dump request 30 <bank> <prog> (0x18008e980) - confirmed on the wire by E382. (4) Cache: {common appdata}/Access Music/Virus TI/Cache/<device id>/<bank name> (0x18009b640, written on bank close 0x18009b8a0). When the 18 bytes from FF7FC0 differ from the registry value "FLSH", "/sync" is added and the user ROM banks are read again (inferred: the stamp changes when the Control Center burns banks). Not found: a SysEx or file source of the user-bank list; a mode switch to Multi/Sequencer on connect (#22 point 2, open) | REA static analysis (sub-agent, addresses above), tirevive-cc info on our TI2, issue #22 report | native/src/access/firmware.cpp, native/src/device/memsync.cpp, src/broker/flashinfo.h | HIGH (structure, bank numbers, paths, factory value 0), MED (a set bit = user-flashed bank: no device with a user bank seen yet), open (Sequencer mode) |

| E388 | 2026-11-02 | USB endpoint types per model, from the USB controller image in the user's firmware.bin (sector F060 of each model image, F061 an identical copy; static, sub-agent, helper scripts work/firmware/scripts/usbdesc*.py, git-ignored; nothing sent to a device). Prompted by issue #26 (a Snow owner on Linux: LIBUSB_ERROR_IO on every transfer, usbfs rejects interrupt URBs on the Snow's endpoints). (1) All three images declare the same device (USB 1.1, 133e:0815, one configuration, 5 interfaces: audio control, EP 0x01 OUT iso 200, EP 0x82 IN iso 592, vendor IF3 and IF4) and the same packet sizes; only the vendor endpoints' type differs: TI (vti.bin) and TI2 (vti_2.bin, = E228): 0x05 OUT 8 / 0x85 IN 16 / 0x06 OUT 32 / 0x86 IN 32 all INTERRUPT, bInterval 1; Snow (vti_snow.bin): the same four BULK. TI and TI2 share a byte-identical F060 ('tusb037.bin'); the Snow has its own build ('tpkt038.bin'), most likely for the same TUSB3200A family (same header layout, same register set, near-same code size). Descriptor offsets in F060: TI/TI2 device +0x0a12, config +0x0a24, vendor EPs +0x0ae1/+0x0aea/+0x0afc/+0x0b05; Snow device +0x0898, config +0x08aa, vendor EPs +0x0967/+0x0970/+0x0982/+0x098b. One descriptor set per image (one device and one config descriptor; fetched by two getters returning fixed addresses: TI2 code 0x1c9c/0x1caa, Snow 0x1a64/0x1a72). (2) Consequence: src/broker/vticore.c uses libusb_interrupt_transfer for the vendor endpoints (reader loop, write_packet), which fits TI/TI2 but not the Snow's bulk endpoints on Linux; the transfer call should follow each endpoint's bmAttributes. Open: the tables say bcdDevice 0x0100 while a real TI2 reports 0x0300 (E099), so code patches at least that field at runtime (not traced; nothing points at the endpoint attributes); not confirmed on a real Snow (lsusb -v asked from the #26 reporter); host-side behaviour of bulk vs interrupt calls on macOS/Windows not checked in source Agrees with E387 (PR #27: lsusb -v and the transfer test on the contributor's Snow); numbered E388 because E387 went to that PR. | static analysis of the user's firmware.bin (descriptor tables, getter code) | src/broker/vticore.c | HIGH (declared types per model), MEDIUM (same chip family), open (hardware confirmation, runtime bcdDevice patch) |

| E392 | 2026-11-04 | What a keyed Virus does, from the firmware (TI2 image only, TI1 and Snow images not read; static reading of work/firmware listings by a subagent plus a read-only linear 8051 decode in the session scratchpad, which can misread data as code; NOTHING run on hardware, no keyed unit tested). Maintainer-session checks of the report: broker sends handshake `4E 73 52 01` (vticore.c:157), 14 replies `73 40 40 00` and none with value 1 in the first 60 captures, firmware strings "Keyboard To MIDI" and "Local On is not reco..." present. The rest is the subagent's reading. (1) Keybed: reaches the DSP over its SHI serial port, not the 8051 (ISR P:$da1, parser 0x52a0a); no strap, the DSP detects the scanner and reports `F4 7F v F7`, host stores it at XDATA 0x04E7 and sets bit 0x04 (v=1 Keyboard, v=2 Polar inferred). (2) Local On (0x40) gates only the internal voice: the message goes out first (0x52cc1 -> 0x52430), then plays internally only if X:$48353 is set (0x52cad, also 0x52d2b); covers notes, wheels, pressure, pedals; incoming MIDI does not use it, so no sound effect on a Desktop (inferred). Setting Local on while remote and keyed queues panel message 0x68 (text not resolved; the firmware has "Local On is not recommended when using VirusControl Plug-In"). (3) Key path: 0x52430 builds a USB-MIDI frame with cable 0x3B into the HI08 ring; host ISR 0x2644 passes it to USB if bit 0x24 is set, else strips the cable and sends it to DIN out: one or the other, never both. Bit 0x24 is set by Mode=3 or 0x52 nonzero. With 3B=1 (E257: cable 0/1 = EP 0x85) keys should arrive on EP 0x85 cable 1, as in E390 (3): static HIGH, still not observed. Panel Destination does not gate keys; DIN in goes to USB when bit 0x24 is set (0x2cfc). (4) Controllers: pitch bend E0, pressure D0, mod wheel CC from 0x43, hold CC from 0x44, pedal 2 CC from 0x45 (0 = off), velocity curve 0x3E, transpose 0x42 on notes only; transpose buttons generate no notes; Channel Mode 1 loops over parts with a per-part flag (Keyboard To MIDI guessed). (5) The handshake's global 0x52: nonzero queues Local = 0, zero queues Local = 1 (0xa83b/0xa84f). So the broker alone turns Local off, before any plug-in; the E022 reply is Local = 0 reported (inferred). Corrects E390 (1), which credited only GlobalInit. (6) Persistence: stores go to RAM 0x04xx/0x05xx, no flash step found (bounded); retained over power-off (inferred, boot mirror compare). Every boot forces 0x52=0, 0x53=0, Local=1 (0x5358); if Mode=3 at boot it queues Mode=0. (7) Master Mode handler 0x0578: entering REMOTE snapshots the globals (FUN_6213), leaving restores 25 of them including Local, cables and 0x52 (FUN_6226, list page 7 0xBF4C). Corrects E391 (2)'s open point. (8) PREDICTION, unproven: TI Revive never leaves REMOTE (E391 (3)), so at the next boot Local is forced on and then Mode=0 restores the snapshot, probably Local off: keys silent standalone after a TI Revive session, which the original avoids with Mode=0 at close. USB suspend (chip event 0x16, meaning inferred) sends 0x52=0, 0x53=0 and may mask this. No watchdog on host silence found (bounded). (9) Reading Local: no single-parameter read, no global dump sender found (bounded); `73 40 40 v` reports are passive; CONFIG page shows Local. Command 0x12 writes globals: never use it to probe. Possible corrections to check, not applied: E252 (bit 7 parameter byte), E331 (4) (link labels swapped). **[Later: simulator S069 confirms v=2 Polar on the model; S096 adds Mode 3 and the port-0 DSP.]** | static, subagent report 2026-11-04; checks above | findings/design-keyed-virus.md, src/broker/vticore.c | MED overall (one reader, 8051 linear sweep) / HIGH (handshake, reply on wire) / LOW (8, prediction) |

B = device object, D = B+0x10 (its interface). CORRECTS E364 (2): the 3072 is not the lead and not the reported latency. (1) Normal mode, reported latency and lead are one number: 0x1800aa090 (vtable +0x98) computes L = base + 3072 (D+0xa60, forced at start, 0x180029af0) and stores L as the stamp lead (D+0xa5c = B+0xa6c); base (0x1800aa000) = 2X + 256 + trunc(block x device/host rate), X = D+0x7e8 from the driver (0x1800ff450), block = D+0x924 if set, else D+0x920 (setBlockSize). It returns L x host/device rate. X = 256 is inferred: it gives 3840 + block, which vtihost printed (protocol.md: 4352 at 512, 4096 at 256). The VST3 shell's getLatencySamples (0x180003f30) returns AEffect.initialDelay unchanged. Calculated at 48 kHz: 32 = 3872 frames 80.7 ms, 256 = 4096 85.3 ms, 512 = 4352 90.7 ms. TI Revive normal mode (virusplugin.h latency_frames, 3072 + max(block,256) + 640): 3968 82.7 ms, 3968 82.7 ms, 4224 88.0 ms, so normal mode is on par. (2) Notes played live skip the lead, per message (stamp 0x1800a8420, notes and F1+ only): stamp = stream position (vtable +0x60) - anchor (B+0xa40) + offset x rate ratio; it adds L only when the status+channel is not in live state and B+0xa70 is not 0 and the offset test passes; otherwise it subtracts 0x400 and the note is 1024 frames in the past, which the DSP plays at once (E247). Two ways in. Flag: effProcessEvents (0x18002ffa0) gives an event with VstMidiEvent flag bit 0 (kVstMidiEventIsRealtime) offset -1. Heuristic: when the host has not declared the flag, each status+channel with offset <= 0 now and on its previous event goes to live state, any offset > 0 leaves it (B+0xa74, B+0xe74). 0x1800a6770 (vtable +0xe0) sets B+0x61028 = heuristic on; 0x18002dc20 at resume sets it to 'host does not declare'; 0x18002d540 sets the declared state from canDo sendVstMidiEventFlagIsRealtime and the host table; the first flagged event turns the heuristic off for good. (3) VST3 shell: its canDo table answers 1 to that string (0x180024850, read at 0x180002997), so in VST3 hosts only flagged notes are live; process (0x1800032e0) passes Event.flags, and Vst2EventList add (0x180001db0) stores it in VstMidiEvent.flags at +0xc, so kIsLive reaches the core. Whether a given host sets kIsLive: not checked. (4) Guard in send path 0x1800a86b0: note-ons reset the marker search's silence counter to 44100; while the search runs (B+0xa50) and its frame count (B+0xa54) is below L, an offset <= 0 becomes 1, so these stay scheduled. (5) Live button: parameter VirusControl/Live Mode (on/off, only when the sequencer is stopped, plugin +0x3c0), listener 0x18002e170. On: lead 0 (B+0xa6c from base only, B+0xa70 = 0), CC 120 on channel 1, the host is told the new delay. With B+0xa70 = 0 the stamp takes the -0x400 branch and no F5 is sent at all, for every note, sequenced ones too. Reported then = 2X, 512 frames, 10.7 ms at 48 kHz (assuming X = 256). Off: lead = VirusControl/Plugin Latency x 1024 (0..3, default 3 = 3072; labels Minimal, Very Low, Low, Normal; no control found for it in the editor.xml of the Mac bundle, the only layout checked). The sync alert ignores events in Live Mode (E205, 0x18006ed70 only stores the reference). Setup Guide: Live is meant for Ableton Live and Sonar; for Cubase, Logic and others it says to lower the buffer or use Direct Monitoring. (6) D button, VirusControl/Direct Monitoring, per part, one part at a time (0x180081980): saves two Output Select parameters of the part, sets both to Out1 L+R, restores them on off or when another part takes it; changing the output by hand ends it. Setup Guide warns that sequenced notes then play early by about a 16th at 120 BPM. OPEN: how this fits the analog hold-back (E344), which 0x180084970 applies to parts below Output Select 9: not traced. (7) TI Revive today (source): no read of kIsLive, no live path, every note gets the lead; both buttons are visible, helptext.cpp says 'Not used by TI Revive yet'; only the sync alert reads Live Mode. | E401 | 2026-11-05 | The original's Audio Unit and Mac core, for #30 (user: 'have a deeper look into the original au'; static only, second reader: a separate agent re-read most claims and corrected several). (1) The AU is a thin wrapper that loads the same core as a VST2 object. Its MIDI entry (AU 0x4bfe) has a second mode: when the host has set property 1013 (AU 0x7a0a; offered as writable, global, 4 bytes in 0x87e0; the name dual scheduling comes from the wrapper's own strings, Apple's header not read), the frame value is masked to 24 bits, offsets are kept non-decreasing, and a note WITHOUT bit 0x1000000 gets the VST realtime flag. Without that property the frame value is passed on and no note is flagged. So in Logic the live path depends on whether Logic sets 1013: not known. (2) The AU's canDo table (0x4ce60) spells the realtime string with a lowercase r, the core asks with a capital R (0x5074a), so the AU answers no; setting 1013 writes the core's byte directly (0xc76e, core +0x4a9). Without 1013 the core falls back to its offset rule (E400). (3) Core processEvents (0x50aa6): live also when the call comes on the thread that created the plug-in (+0x118, 0x54a1a). Channel pressure is merged per call and sent at the highest stamp. Per the second reader CC, bend, aftertouch and program get no stamp of their own, they inherit the last F5, while TI Revive stamps them all, so a notes-only live path would leave wheels late; note-on and note-off state are separate, a scheduled off could land after the next live on (not tested). (4) Host table 0x1bb080 (0x98 bytes per entry, first match wins): only Ableton Live from 5000 and Digital Performer from 6000 are marked as declaring the realtime flag; Logic is not. Ableton gets two vendor calls ('AbLi' 5 at open with a log line about older versions refusing realtime flags, 'AbLi' 6 at resume with the delay and 2 x block; 0x54a1a, 0x52b0a). Meaning of op 6 not known; VST2 only. (5) USB buffer X: on the Mac the master asks for 256, 128 at VST block <= 128, and 64/128/256 by an IO block when one is known (0xf0620), then takes what the driver grants (0xf0803, second reader). On Windows the master asks a constant 128 (0x1800a995c/71/89, second reader). In the AU the VST block is MaximumFramesPerSlice (0x8f86), not the I/O buffer; TI Revive too (auwrapper.mm:899), and Logic runs 1024/2048 (E365), so TI Revive's formula gives 99 and 120 ms there at 48 kHz, whatever the I/O buffer. Custom property 101010 that would pass the real I/O size is set by nothing in the AU. (6) Live notes still pay the output ring (base, 0xeceac, second reader), so X and block matter when playing. Live Mode reports 2X but nothing found shrinks the ring below base. On stop, note-ons on channels 1-15 go live when the offset rule is on (0xec11e, second reader, partly inferred). (7) Setup warnings (0x505f2, 0x50d84, 0x5051a, popup 0x4f336): block above 512, block not a power of two, and for Logic or the AU bridge a timing check that asks for Process Buffer Range Small. TI Revive has no such hints. Preference UseLuxuryLatency, default on: off means no lead and no F5 while L is still reported (second reader). Not checked: Apple's header, what Logic does, Windows transport handling. | REA ev_35c31b74 (AU MIDI entry), ev_1426fcca (SetProperty), ev_c87ae492 (GetPropertyInfo), ev_2dd25e01 (host detect, processEvents, resume, vendor calls), ev_d1aacbfd (host table), ev_0fb35566 (warnings, base, resume), ev_e82cbaa1 (timing check); AU x86_64 slice sha256 4bdea3b9.., Mac core x86_64 slice sha256 52bd4a7d.. (6) In Live the tutorial routes MIDI tracks to the plug-in's track and needs Record Arm. | E429 | 2026-11-06 | First findings taken from the private simulator (virus-ti-sim S entries), after an independent review agent that did not see how they were made checked them against firmware listings and stored captures, using its own linear 8051 decode because REA has no 8051. Items (3) and (5) were also measured by the main session, the rest come from that review and were not repeated. Simulator behaviour alone is not evidence about the hardware, so only the parts with a real source are here. (1) Dumps can lose exactly 256 packets when a second reply is written while the first is unsent: 5 of 1334 real dumps in 5 captures of the original are 281 bytes, each where the original asked for it twice about 0.46 s apart, then asks again about 9.5 s later and gets it whole; the writers at 0x22b0 and 0x22df advance index 0x3D with no compare against 0x3E (HIGH seen, MED mechanism). TI Revive's memsync sends one request per instance and checks size and fingerprint; two clients at once not checked. (2) `73 xx 54 01` comes as part 0x40 on EP 0x85 cable 1 (505 real) or part 0 on EP 0x86 cable 2 (4657 real), never both in one capture, so E251's "it is 0" does not hold for every session (HIGH that it varies; which state decides it comes from a model run, MED). (3) Marker distance on the real TI2 is not a constant: `ping at N, M frames later` in work/hil/*/H*.err, 219 lines, 498 to 8852 frames, median 2008, 110 above 2000; at most about 201 ms at 44.1 kHz. The review counted 167 lines, median 946, on a narrower set of files, not reconciled (HIGH spread). (4) After the plug-in sends a dump to the edit buffer the read-back differs in bytes 11 and 12 in 16 of 18 captures, and also in 10, 265, 522 in the other 2: these are E156's Original Bank and Original Patch, rewritten by the Virus, so read-back comparisons ignore bytes 10 to 12 (HIGH). (5) Left and right of H1 recordings on the real TI2 are aligned, correlation 1.00000 at lag 0 in 20261106-065445 and 071108; the simulator's own one-frame left delay is a model error, not hardware behaviour. The marker's one-frame delay in protocol.md is not touched by this (HIGH for those recordings). (6) At 0x6387 the firmware clears bit 0x24 and sets globals 0x3A, 0x3B, 0x3C back to 1, reached from 0x0612 and 0x176c when bit 0x13 is clear and global 0x52 is 0 (HIGH static, LOW effect on hardware: no capture tests it). (7) Host port words are written value, parameter, part at registers 5, 6, 7 (0x6511), which with HLEND gives E252's order (HIGH). (8) The USB chip's code at 0x03b8 to 0x03c7 and 0x038e to 0x039a sends the link frames for global 0x53 after a debounce of 20 ticks; whether a real chip does this without a PC is open (HIGH code, MED meaning). The other S entries are not taken yet, status open. | review agent's static reading, captures via the simulator's extractor (not independently verified against usbdecode.py), work/hil logs and recordings | - | as stated per item |

By the ST product matrix (a search result, datasheet text not read): 24 MHz grade, 3.3 V, 256K main flash, 32K secondary, 32K SRAM, 12-clock 8032 core. (2) Crystal against that CPU marked FT24.576 with GMKDD below (TI2-B IMG_4453, line two also read by the maintainer); traces not followed. Fits 24.576 MHz / 12 = 2.048 MHz, S004's fit, but the host clock stays open: the simulator agent reports (S004, S042) that round timer counts argue for 2.000 MHz, that the crystal's traces are not followed, and that the uPSD325x datasheet gives no 31250 baud from these settings; settling it needs the traces or DIN's measured bit time. It leaves E230's DIN baud rate open: TH1 0xFC at that clock gives 16000 or 32000 baud, not 31250, so what clocks DIN is unexplained. The second crystal, beside the USB chip, reads 6.000 with a second line about 7? 9F (photo enlarged and sent by the maintainer 2026-11-06, on TI2-B's layout; the second line is not fully legible); traces not followed. The TUSB3200A data manual SLES018B gives a 6 MHz crystal, PLL 48 MHz, MCU 12 or 24 MHz by GLOBCTL bit 7 (read by the simulator agent, S043, not by the main session); its model shows the firmware writes GLOBCTL C5, so 24 MHz, 2.000 MHz machine cycle. (3) USB chip: TI TUSB3200AC, both boards. (4) DSPs: 2x Freescale DSPB56367AG150 K41R, all boards seen; date codes vary. (5) Codec: Cirrus CS42516-CQZ, Desktop and Polar. (6) External flash on the back: Spansion S29AL032D70TFI00, 32 Mbit by its name, sector layout not checked. (7) Static RAM: Cypress CY62148EV30LL-45ZSXI, 512K x 8 per distributor listings, on the edge by the panel connector with 74LV573A and 74LVC245A beside it, so probably the host's external memory (inference, not traced); Alliance AS7C34096A-10TIN by the DSPs on Desktop boards, Samsung K6R4 on the Polar. (8) Also NE5532, HC574, 74HC4052, 74HC04, 74HC4053 (read by eye). Board print VIRM_D25 TOP, (C) KEMPER DIGITAL on the back. So E416's first-generation layout holds for a TI2 in the places visible. | E439 | 2026-11-07 | **Correction, same day: Linux only, the "and macOS" below was wrong.** The Mac broker does not compile vtiusbaudio_libusb.c (Makefile target `mac`; a build at 55147d4 has no "stream stalled" string, nor does the installed one); macOS audio is Core Audio, and native/src/device/audioin.cpp's gap fill follows the device's mSampleTime, not wall clock. Windows (src/broker/vtiusbaudio.c `submit_read`) takes gaps from USB frame numbers. Both by reading, neither run for this. Original entry: Possible fault in TI Revive's own broker on libusb (Linux and macOS), by reading, not yet shown on a real unit; found by the simulator agent, read and confirmed in the source by the main session. src/broker/vtiusbaudio_libusb.c `in_cb`: 8 transfers of 10 ms stay queued, about 80 ms; a stall is decided by wall clock only, `behind = (now - t0) - (delivered + NP)`, and above 30 ms fills `behind` ms of silence and adds it to `delivered_ms`. If the thread handling libusb events is held up about 30-80 ms while transfers stay queued, no frames were lost, yet silence is added, then the queued frames arrive too, so the frame counter stays ahead of the synth by the fill for the rest of the session, every later note late by that, spread 0, with `stream stalled` in the broker's log. Same shape as the simulator's late class (S058, 31-41 ms stalls). E078's reason for the fill was real lost frames. Not seen in 55 short real streams of E435-E438. **But it does fire on the real unit, by another route, found after the maintainer remembered a session cured by restarting the plug-in:** this PC's broker journal has `stream stalled, 30 ms filled with silence` 5 times since 2026-10-25, in long 48 kHz sessions: 22.5 min after audio on and again every 22.4-22.7 min (17:08:45, 17:31:12, 17:53:38 on 10-25; 06:26:17, 06:48:59 on 11-05). 30 ms per 22.4 min is 22 ppm, fitting hil's measured drift of -3 to -31 ppm: the wall clock runs ahead of the Virus' clock, so `behind` grows steadily with no stall at all, and at 30 ms silence is inserted that no lost frame explains. **Measured on the real TI2, 2026-11-07, 48 kHz, block 64, rc3, lead 3072, a C4 each second for 26 min (work/latency/drift):** `stream stalled, 30 ms filled` at 15:35:04, 22.7 min in; every note exactly 3968 frames until note 1364, then from note 1365 on 5408, +1440 frames = exactly 30 ms, to the end, one note 4928; the plug-in's reported delay is unchanged and it does not take the 30 ms back. So in a session longer than about 22 min on this unit the Virus plays 30 ms later than reported, and more each time. Restarting cures it. Direction depends on the two clocks, so other units may never hit it or hit it at other times. **Fix bdc906f, src/broker/stallfill.h, measured on the real TI2 with a dev broker (586ba7182ada4e74), plug-in rc3, 48 kHz, block 64:** 26 min run: no `stream stalled` from drift, notes 3968 past 22.7 min; a 60 ms SIGSTOP of the broker at 5 min changed nothing. A 300 ms SIGSTOP at 8 min filled 233 ms, and in that run notes then stayed 7872, +81 ms, to the end, about the transfer queue's length, not explained. The same 300 ms pause in 4 short 180 s runs, pause at 100 s: old broker filled 304, 300; new filled 230, 227 (close to 220 lost); all 4 had one late note and then 3968 again. So the plug-in or the stream recovers from such a gap in these runs on either broker, and the fix does not make it worse; the +81 is 1 of 5 pauses, seen after 8 min and an earlier pause, open. Note 1398 read 4928 in both long runs, on either broker, unexplained, maybe the test. Unit tests: 5 new, seen failing with the old rule, 567 pass. macOS not built or run with the fix; Windows path not read for it. Whether any user's report is this is not known. | E449 | 2026-11-07 | How the original handles a live note, Direct Monitoring, Live Mode and Plugin Latency (static, Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64 sha256 a622f9a2.., REA/Ghidra, one agent's reading, not re-read by a second, nothing run). B = device object, D = B+0x10, C = core, P = plug-in. (1) Live note after sequenced ones, 0x1800a8420, assembly: it sends a new, LOWER F5, nothing clamps or inherits. Stamp = device position (+0x60) - anchor (B+0xa40) + offset x ratio; sequenced adds the lead, live is that minus 0x400 (flagged events get offset -1 in 0x18002ffa0). F5 is left out only when position and offset both equal the previous message's. The only order check is on raw stamps, before lead and -0x400 (0x1800a84fe-8552): raw going backward logs and restarts the marker search (0x1800a8880), only when B+0xa4 = 0, which means playing (MED). Note-off takes the same path. CC, bend, aftertouch, program leave at 0x1800a8452 with no stamp, inheriting the last F5 (confirms E401 (3) on Windows): after a sequenced note they wait with it, after a live note they play at once. E235 (not re-read): a late F5 ends the queued block and its bytes play at once, so a live note could pull queued sequenced notes forward; not measured. (2) Direct Monitoring, the per-part `D`, listener 0x180081980, branch 0x180083584-0x18008374c, decompiled view, HIGH: no SysEx of its own; it saves the part's two Output Select parameters, sets both to Out1 L+R, restores them on off or when another part takes over; changing them by hand clears D. Exact wire messages not traced, assumed ordinary part parameters. Not stored in the project, inferred, MED. Analog hold, 0x18002fa30, assembly: only at transport edges; Live Mode on or stopped gives 0, otherwise two unidentified slots + 512 + 2 x block samples, sent via 0x18002f9f0 and 0x180084970; so a D part should get `6F pp 7F ms` while playing, inferred; conflicts with the Setup Guide's words, not measured; closes E400's open point only in part. (3) Live Mode and Plugin Latency, 0x18002e170, assembly, HIGH: Live on: C+0xcd020 = 0, D+0xa5c = D+0xa60 = 0 (0x1800aa1a0), reports 2X (0x1800aa090), `B0 78 00`, then no lead and no F5 for anything, sequenced notes too, analog hold 0 from the next transport edge. Live off or a latency change: `B0 78 00`, again at the next process (P+0x3ca), C+0xcd020 = N << 10, reported only if changed; re-applied at device restart (0x180070520). Plugin Latency = N x 1024 frames: 0, 21.3, 42.7, 64 ms at 48 kHz on top of base; Minimal (0) behaves like Live, inferred from the same branch [NOT SUPPORTED by E453]. Applied only with Live off (0x18002e2ca) while the XML allows editing only with Live on, so it takes effect when Live is turned off. 'Stopped only' is XML alone. Not established: the audible effect of any of this, Sync Information Events from lower F5s, Mac core not re-read. Corrects the HANDOFF design note that StampFloor's never-backwards rule must be kept for live notes: the original does not keep it. **[Later: simulator S079 supports E449 (3) against E453 (MED).]** | REA static analysis, session closed, agent's report | E235, E251, E344, E400, E401, E402 | HIGH/MED per item as stated, single reader; open (everything on the real unit) |

It is the 'luxury latency': an extra lead of 0 / 1024 / 2048 / 3072 frames (Minimal .. Normal) added to the reported latency. FUN_18002e170: a change with Live Mode off sends B0 78 00, stores N << 10 at C+0xcd020 (0x18002e342), calls slot 0x48; Live Mode on stores 0 (0x18002e1fa), slot 0x48 with 0, B0 78 00; both report a new latency (slots 0xe0, 0x1e0). Slot 0x48 = the LuxuryLatencyOffset setter; at device start FUN_18006ddf0 reads the stored preferences UseTimeStamps (0x38), UseLuxuryLatency (0x40), LuxuryLatencyOffset (0x48) and UseSampleRateConverter (0x50). Reported latency = (luxury + base) x a factor (Control Center's getInitialDelay copy 0x140022590); no buffer resized on that path. No control binds the parameter in the plug-in's XML (only a lookup by name, 0x18002c3da) and no 'Plugin Latency' string in the Control Center, but the Control Center HAS the luxury names (UseLuxuryLatency, LuxuryLatencyOffset, 'Desired luxury latency (after conversion)', file offsets 0x74333-0x743cb): so the setting most likely lives in the Control Center's stored preferences, inferred. CORRECTS E449 (3): 'Minimal behaves like Live, no F5' is not supported: Minimal only sets the extra lead to 0; F5 stamps hang on the separate preference UseTimeStamps, not checked elsewhere. TI Revive 9ca64a0 follows the old reading (timing_mode: N = 0 -> no stamps); no control reaches N, so nothing triggers it; to fix before the value gets a control. The XML condition (active with Live Mode on) and the code (applied with Live Mode off) still disagree, not resolved. | E472 | 2026-11-08 | Mac dropouts after sleep: the cause is macOS's USB audio path, not TI Revive. MacBook Air M3, macOS 26.6.2, TI2 Desktop, 0.8.39 CI build, Ableton Live, by the user with Claude reading diag.log, the broker log and pmset. (1) After sleeps (16:55, 17:15) and a fresh Live start after a sleep (17:08, 'filled 656': Core Audio delivered gaps the plug-in filled), silent gaps in the Virus' audio through the DAW that did not settle; a USB replug cleared them each time (17:08:45, later again). (2) After a power cycle of the Virus with the USB cable left in (17:26), the plug-in anchored on a late sync answer (marker 5318 frames after the ping, normally about 500-1000) and read margin min stayed 0 in every window (reads clamped all the time, 'filled 1308'): dropouts; a Live restart then anchored normally (marker 678, margin 64, drops 0, filled 0), yet the dropouts went on. (3) The Virus' headphones were clean throughout; a QuickTime recording of the 'Virus TI' Core Audio input, with no TI Revive in the audio path, had the same gaps; a software synth in Live was clean. So after a Mac sleep (and after a Virus power cycle with the cable attached) macOS's USB audio stream for the Virus can stay gapped until the USB device is re-enumerated (replug). A note also hung on the Virus (audible on its headphones) that two panics did not end; a USB replug did not end it, a power cycle did. Since 0.8.39's live-notes default, controllers not from the DAW (the panic's CC 123) inherit the last F5 instead of their own stamp (inherits_stamp), which after a disturbed timeline may place them wrong: not shown to be the cause of the hanging note, to fix anyway. Earlier rounds' Mac sleep passed (E215, E397): why it differs now is not known (macOS update or chance). Possible remedy, not built: after a detected sleep the broker resets the Virus' USB port (a known operation, the Linux broker does it at give-back) so macOS re-enumerates it; to try on our own Virus first. Plus: the plug-in should not keep a late-anchored timeline (marker far after the ping) without re-pinging. | the user's tests and the Mac's logs | native/src/device/audioin.cpp, native/src/device/stamprule.h inherits_stamp | HIGH (where the gaps arise) / open (why, and the remedy) |

Proposal from the simulator, not decided: a plug-in detector plus an automatic logged stream restart. **Detector 2026-11-09 (simulator S120, all captures):** the slip is an exact map (byte 2k = high byte of word k, byte 2k+1 = low byte of word (k+2) mod 6; undoing it on s070-59 gives a normal stream with the marker). Rule: per half-second window the sum of byte changes at the high-byte positions over the sum at both; slipped streams >= 0.991 in every window (4 of 4), normal and 'other' at most 0.5 in 977 streams (the high ones are single full-scale glitches); words 0/1 are the analog inputs (ADC noise floor in 940 streams, not a constant). Through a float gain or a resampler a slip falls to 0.26-0.33, so the rule needs the device's own bytes; on the Mac our ring is rebuilt from Core Audio and channels 0/1 read exactly 0 (not the ADC offset), so the Mac only logs. Built in 0.8.41 (slipwatch.h, threshold 0.9, two windows in a row, restart through the broker at most 5 a session 10 s apart, option slip-restart on by default): on the real TI2 a normal session raised nothing and VTI_NATIVE_FAKE_SLIP ran the restart (stop and start 5 ms apart, new marker, every note exact). Whether a restart cures a real slip is still not observed.  **Correction (simulator's review of the Mac diag logs, work/timing/mac-0.8.30/-0.8.31/-0.8.31c):** the Mac sessions ran in IO configuration 2 (the sidechain, which TI Revive sends only with usb_io = 2, virusplugin.cpp:2147), where words 0/1 carry the silent USB 3 (E158/E164); at 'device connected' they read '4 2', the ADC offsets. So channels 0/1 = 0 on the Mac is the configuration, not a non-1:1 Core Audio path; the slip rule may hold there too. Control c (3 sessions, steady host without --rough, Play at 23.97, s068ctl c-1..3): notes 20-29 all exactly 3840 in 3 of 3, so Play alone moves nothing. The rough cadence is a function of the block index only (hostclock.h shift_us), so Play cannot reshuffle it; the +384 then looks like Play together with batched blocks (inferred, 2 sessions), it matters only for hosts that batch blocks. **Traced 2026-11-09 (simulator, s068 and s068ctl pcapngs + .err; an 'aligned blocks' reading was withdrawn as a stats-window artefact of the 8-block bursts):** on the wire every note of s068-1 and notes 24/25 of s068ctl stand exactly on their stamps, and the unit sounded each note on its stamp (iso IN onset gaps = stamp gaps), while the WAV has the late notes (+256..+1408, and the +384 after Play). So ALL the lateness with --rough is added on the plug-in's read side. Place (read, checked by us): native/plugin/source/virusplugin.cpp:1889-1903 (v0.8.40): the read position block_start + lead() - latency_frames() does not depend on Play, but when a burst of batched blocks runs ahead of the audio that has arrived, the read is pulled back to avail - n (read margin min 0 in every rough session), so the sound comes late by the shortfall until the audio catches up; clamp_watch re-anchors only after a full second of clamping, so short clamps are not counted anywhere. The +384 after Play is then most likely one more such clamp (guess: Play only nudges the burst's timing; 3 sessions cannot separate that from chance). Matters for hosts that batch blocks. Possible TI Revive work, not decided: count pulled-back blocks per stats window, then a larger margin while bursts are seen, or a read that follows the burst. **Measured 2026-11-09 with the new burst counter** (round-0.8.41 local build, 'stats: pulled back N blocks, largest S frames', work/latency/burst, 48 kHz, block 256, default lead, fresh broker, 30 s): steady host 2 of 2 sessions with no pulled-back block and every note exact; --rough 0:30 2 of 2 sessions with 446-683 pulled-back blocks per 10 s window (about a third of all blocks), largest shortfall 1608-3237 frames, and the known late notes (up to +2432). So the read clamp is confirmed as the cause of the lateness with batched blocks. | nativehost WAVs, latency_measure.py, simulator's per-note reading (C6, C7) | S068, S097, E489 | HIGH (as measured; the read clamp confirmed by the counter)
| E491 | 2026-11-09 | The original pings again within a session (for S094, static REA/Ghidra 12.1.4 on the user's Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64, sha256 a622f9a2...; evidence ev_c6afd9f2, ev_16c7c4c1, ev_b670a4a6, ev_a852277c, ev_aa8e5548, ev_1fa711ee). The sync ping (the 11-byte `F0 00 20 33 01 10 73 00 22 00 F7`, data 0x1801c5690) is sent from exactly one place, 0x1800a8aa0, called from the audio callback 0x1800a8f60 while a sync is armed. Sending waits for quiet: the routine counts down about one second of frames (0xac44 = 44100) and restarts the count whenever any sample of the Virus' channels 2-5 in the block is louder than about 1/16 of full scale (0x80000 in its 24-bit-in-32 samples); only when the count runs out does it send the ping and start looking for the marker (0x1800a8900). Arming (0x1800a8880, with a delay and a search window in ms) happens: at the first stream start (250 ms delay, 40 s window); when the marker search window ends without a marker (retry: 1 s of quiet again, then a new ping); when the host's sample position jumps while a marker is awaited (0x1800a8f60, 1 s / 1 s); when, outside a sync, the first sample of the marker channel pair is loud (1 s / 3 s; reading MED: which pair 0x820/0x828 is has not been followed); when a stamp comes out earlier than the previous one (0x1800a8420, unless a flag at +0xa4 is set); when a mode flag is switched off (0x1800a8370, reached only indirectly, 250 ms / 50 ms; which mode is a guess: Live Mode, E457); and after every sample-rate switch (E323). So a second ping in the middle of a session is a known behaviour of the original, always sent after about 1 s with the Virus' outputs quiet, and the stamp clock restarts with it (E246). Consequence: a TI Revive test switch for S094 that re-syncs once mid-session, sent only after 1 s of quiet output like the original, is within rule 2. | REA static analysis | E246, E323, E457, S094, S095 | HIGH (one send site, the quiet countdown, the arming calls) / MED (the meaning of the marker-pair and mode-flag triggers) |

All later sessions the same: 48 kHz kept broker (12 sessions), then a fresh broker at 48 kHz and at 44.1 kHz. Simulator scan of 1039 streams: live runs under 0.05 s.  **Expert review (simulator, 2026-11-09, logs only):** the frozen session's ping went out at 17:53:44.702 and no marker came at all (real markers come at most about 0.14-0.2 s after a ping), also not in the 5.6 s before the frames froze, while words 0/1 still carried live ADC noise; so the marker-rendering DSP or the USB 2 path had stopped about 2.7 s BEFORE the constant frames (inferred, LOW-MED: the DSPs stopped and their serial port kept repeating its last words; the reviewer also counts 0 sync reports in the frozen sessions, not reproduced). E112 (Mac, a freeze after a rate change) may be the same. Next time before a power cycle: does the tempo LED still blink, does a knob still send a CC (both come from the DSPs). **Likely cause (simulator, the frozen capture's unit-to-host messages):** the frozen session r48000-b64-a640-s1 got from the Virus `73 40 40 00`, then `73 00 10 00` (global 0x10 System Clock/Frequency = 44.1 kHz) 0.27 s after the stream start, while the stream ran at 48 kHz (576-byte packets), and after it no DSP-origin message at all (no sync report). The good 48 kHz sessions report `73 00 10 01` (freeze/s2-r48000, the same kept-broker 44.1 -> 48 step: twice; l9 r48000-b64-a896-s1) and a sync report `73 00 54 01` about 4 s later. Inferred (MED): the unit's host CPU kept 44.1 kHz for the DSPs while the USB chip and the stream ran at 48 kHz, so the DSPs ran on the wrong rate and stopped: E331's divergence path (the host CPU can set the DSP rate from its stored global 0x10 without the chip). Why once and not the next time: open (a race at the kept-broker rate change?). The broker's relock (ratesw.h) does not see it: it reacts to packets of the other rate, and here the packets were right.
